does not have any eigenvalues (embedded or not) and
\sigma(L_{k})=\sigma_{ess}(L_{k})=[v(\vartheta_{1}),v(\vartheta_{2})]
.

Its proof is presented in Section
8
and consists in showing that
(L_{k}-\lambda)
is continuously invertible for all
\lambda\not\in[v(\vartheta_{1}),v(\vartheta_{2})]
. To further understand the invertibility properties of
(L_{k}-\lambda)
, we now need to investigate, among others, the eigenvalue problem

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{k}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-\lambda}+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-\lambda)^{2}}\right)\psi

\displaystyle=0

(2.26)

\displaystyle\psi|_{y=0,2}

\displaystyle=0,

(2.27)

with
\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}
. To that purpose, we make use of the main properties of the homogeneous solutions (
2.25
) to the Taylor-Goldstein equation introduced in Section
7
in order to construct eigenfunction candidates. For instance,

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(y)=\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0})

is an eigenfunction of (
2.26
) of eigenvalue
\lambda=v(y_{0})\mp i\varepsilon
if
\varphi_{k}(0)=0
. In Section
8
we show that
\varphi_{k}(0)\neq 0
if H2 holds and
\varepsilon>0
is small enough. Standard energy estimates and H1 further show that
\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\rm Re}(\lambda)\not\in[v(\vartheta_{1}),v(\vartheta_{2})]
is in the resolvent of
L_{k}
. The last case of
\lambda\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\rm Im}(\lambda)\neq 0
and
{\rm Re}(\lambda)\in[v(\vartheta_{1}),v(\vartheta_{2})]
is treated with a connectedness argument that involves a limiting procedure towards the linearised Euler equations inspired by
[
sinambela2025transition
]
and a detailed understanding of the homogeneous solutions (
2.25
) in the fragile, weak, mild and strong regimes, which is carried out in Sections
7
,
8
and
9
.

2.7.
Sobolev regularity of the generalized stream-function

Once the coercive estimate (
2.18
) is established, we now obtain
Z_{k}
regularity of
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
in terms of regularity of
w_{k}^{0}
and
q_{k}^{0}
thanks to the mapping properties (
2.21
) of the solutions operators
R_{m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
for
m=0,1
.

In view of (
2.4
), we are also interested in regularity of
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
in order to use oscillatory-phase arguments to obtain decay in time. Now,

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{k}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=v^{\prime}(y_{0})\left(\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}-2\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{3}}\right)\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{0})w_{k}^{0}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}

and we readily observe that
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\in X_{k}
is not enough to ensure that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\left(\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}-2\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{3}}\right)\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

is uniformly bounded, since
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
is not enough to compensate the cubic singularity. However, for

\displaystyle\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=\left(\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}\right)\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

we readily have that
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. Since we have a proper understanding of
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
in
Z_{k}
, we deduce from there the main regularity properties of
\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. More importantly,
\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
is now the solution to

\begin{split}\text{TG}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}&=\frac{v^{\prime\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}-\frac{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\\
&\quad+2{\mathcal{P}}(y)\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{3}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}-v^{\prime\prime}(y)\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\\
&\quad+\frac{\partial_{y}\omega_{k}^{0}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}-\omega_{k}^{0}(y)\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}+\partial_{y}\varrho_{k}^{0}(y),\end{split}

(2.28)

with now

\displaystyle\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0})=\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0}),\quad\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0})=\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0}).

The limiting absorption principle then shows that the
X_{k}
bounds of
\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are bounded by the
X_{k}
bounds of the Green’s function acting on the source term of (
2.28
). A power law counting argument shows that this action may now be bounded: the regularity structures of
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, in fact that of the coefficients
({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm})_{\sigma}
and
(\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm})_{\sigma}
, may compensate the most singular factors, which are morally at most quadratic singularities. The estimates on the regularity properties of the source term are carried out in Section
E
and
F
. They are then used in Section
12
to obtain the regularity of
\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and thus also of
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. The same idea proves also useful to describe
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, where now one must study
\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}=(\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}})\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
.

We remark here that
\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}
is sometimes referred to in the literature as the ”good derivative” in that, while not fully commuting with the differential operator (the Taylor-Goldstein here and the Rayleigh operator in the Euler equations) it does not increase the singularities of the equation and one can hope to close the relevant estimates. We refer the interested reader to
[
BCZV19
,
WZZ18
,
WZZ19
,
WZZKolmo20
,
IJ22
,
IIJ22
]
for other instances where the good derivative has been used.

2.8.
Linear inviscid damping of the perturbed density and velocity field

The regularity structures for
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm},\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
encoded in their
X_{k}
estimates are next used to show that the perturbed velocity field and density given by (
2.4
) experience time-decay by means of oscillatory-phase methods: time decay is obtained at the expenses of integrability of
\partial_{y_{0}}
derivatives. For the velocity field
v_{k}=(-\partial_{y}\psi_{k},ik\psi_{k})
it is immediate to see from the
X_{k}
definition that the regularities of
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm},\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
depend on the local Richardson number
{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})
and that, more importantly,
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\partial_{y,y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are in general not uniformly integrable as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

This has two related consequences when estimating the velocity field
v_{k}(t,y)
. First, the regularity of
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
and
\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
is dictated by
\mu(y_{0})
near the critical layer. Precisely since
y_{0}\approx y
, we can actually assume that the regularity of
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi(y,y_{0})
and
\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
is in fact determined by
\mu(y)
and thus essentially uniform near the critical layer: it is uniform in the strongly stratified regime, it experiences a logarithmic loss in the mild regime, becomes weaker in the weak regime and it eventually degenerates as the spectral parameter
y_{0}
approaches the stratification boundaries
\vartheta_{1}
and
\vartheta_{2}
.

Secondly, we need to consider the integrability of
\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
both near the critical layer and away from it. In the critical layer they are locally integrable, and thus small (depending on
\mu(y)
) on a small subset of the critical layer. On the other hand, we can integrate by parts once more in the complement of the small subset to obtain an extra
t^{-1}
decay, at the expenses of getting closer and closer to the critical layer, where
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\partial_{y,y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
become singular (also depending on
\mu(y)
). Balancing out the size of the subset gives the claimed decay estimates in Theorem
B
and is carried out in Section
13
.

The proof of Theorem
C
is also done in Section
13
and hinges on two observations. The first one is that for
y_{0}
in the non-stratified regime, that is
{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})=0
, the Taylor-Goldstein operator becomes the Rayleigh operator for the linearised Euler equations, which is one power less singular than the Taylor-Goldstein operator. In particular, the solution
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
to (
2.16
) is now more regular, and we can argue as in
[
Jia20
,
JiaGev20
]
to show that for
y\in[0,\vartheta_{1}]\cup[\vartheta_{2},2]
, the non-stratified region, the velocity field
v_{k}(t,y)
experiences Euler-type inviscid damping decay rates, which are comparatively faster than the Boussinesq rates. Secondly, for
y
close to
\vartheta_{2}
, say, we are able to use the fundamental theorem of calculus to relate
\psi_{k}(t,y)
with
\psi_{k}(t,\vartheta_{2})
and a slower time-decaying contribution which vanishes as
y\rightarrow\vartheta_{2}
.

2.9.
Sub-linear growth of vorticity and gradient of density

Once the decay estimates of Theorem
B
are available, we can use them to obtain Theorem
D
. More precisely, from (
2.4
) we now have

\displaystyle\omega_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{2}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

(2.29)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{2}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

(2.30)

According to the regularity properties of
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and (
2.16
), we observe that
\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\partial_{y}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}\right)
are no longer uniformly integrable as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. To overcome this singularity, we now integrate backwards, noting here that
\partial_{y}=\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}-\partial_{y_{0}}
and
\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}
is in general more regular. This backwards integration produces powers of
t
when
\partial_{y_{0}}
acts on the oscillatory factors and, after suitable optimization on the size of the small set, is the main reason behind the sub-linear growth of
\omega(t,x,y)
and
\partial_{y}\rho(t,x,y)
. Just as in Theorem
B
, the growth estimates obtained this way degenerate as
y\rightarrow\vartheta_{1}
or
y\rightarrow\vartheta_{2}
. Nonetheless, we can use again the fundamental theorem of calculus and the behaviour of
\omega(t,x,y)
and
\partial_{y}\rho(t,x,y)
in the non-stratified regions to locally improve the growth bounds, which culminate in Theorem
E
after suitably regularizing the governing equations for
\omega_{k}(t,y)
and
\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)
. The proofs of Theorem
D
and
E
are carried out in Section
14
.

We remark here that Theorem
D
can also be proved integrating the linearised equations (
2.1
) and using the decay estimates for
\psi_{k}(t,y)
,
\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)
and
\rho_{k}(t,y)
obtained in Section
13
. While we opted for studying (
2.29
) and (
2.30
) above since they are later needed to improve the growth bounds to Theorem
E
, we give further details on how to use (
2.1
) to prove the growth bounds in Remark
14.9
below.

3.
Green’s function of the RTG operator

In this section we give the explicit formulas for
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)
that will be used in the manuscript. Central to the study of the Green’s function are the Whittaker functions
[
Whittaker03
]
, a class of hyper-geometric functions that satisfy equations of the form

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}M_{\kappa,\gamma}+\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\kappa}{\zeta}+\frac{\tfrac{1}{4}-\gamma^{2}}{\zeta^{2}}\right)M_{\kappa,\gamma}=0,\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}

for
\kappa,\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Their regularity properties, reported in Appendix
B
, are fundamental to explain the long-time behaviour of solutions to the linearised system and the precise form of the time-decay rates presented in Theorem
B
. In this manuscript we use the Whittaker functions with
\kappa=0
and
\gamma=\mu+i\nu\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
\mu\nu=0
. For
\zeta\in C
, we define
M_{\mathsf{r}}(\xi):=M_{0,\gamma}\left(2k\xi\right)
and
M_{\mathsf{s}}(\xi):=M_{0,-\gamma}\left(2k\xi\right)
, which solve the (re-scaled) Whittaker equation

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}M_{\sigma}+\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\tfrac{1}{4}-\gamma^{2}}{4k^{2}\zeta^{2}}\right)M_{\sigma}=0,\quad\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}.

(3.1)

When
\gamma=0
then
M_{\mathsf{r}}
and
M_{\mathsf{s}}
are no longer linearly independent. To overcome this difficulty, we define
W_{\mathsf{r}}(\zeta):=W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)
to be the unique solution to (
3.1
) such that

\displaystyle W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\zeta}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma}U(\tfrac{1}{2}+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta),

where
U(a,b,\zeta)
denotes the Kummer function uniquely determined by the property that
U(a,b,\zeta)\approx\zeta^{-a}
for
\zeta\rightarrow\infty
. While we shall give more precise details on these hyper-geometric functions in Appendix
B
, we now state our main formulae for the Green’s function of (
2.7
).

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. The Green’s function
{\mathcal{G}}_{m,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
of
\textsc{TG}_{m,\varepsilon}^{\pm}

is given by

{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\begin{cases}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z),\quad&0\leq z\leq y\leq 2,\\
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z),\quad&0\leq y\leq z\leq 2,\end{cases}

(3.2)

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0}),

(3.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})

(3.4)

where
\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})
are two homogeneous solutions to

\left(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2}}\right)\phi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=0

(3.5)

such that

\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0})=0
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0})=0
, respectively, for all
y_{0}\in[0,2]
.

•

For
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
, they are given by

\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

(3.6)

and

\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

(3.7)

with Wronskian

{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0}):=-4k\gamma_{0}\Big(M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\Big).

(3.8)

•

For
y_{0}\in I_{M}
in the mildly stratified region, they are given by

\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-W_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

(3.9)

and

\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-W_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

(3.10)

with Wronskian

{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0}):=-2k\frac{\Gamma(1+2\gamma)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma\right)}\Big(M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-W_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\Big).

(3.11)

Furthermore, we have the relation
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0},z)=\overline{{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0},z)}
, for all
y,y_{0},z\in[0,2]
.

The proof of the proposition is rather standard: The Green’s functions is constructed by first solving the boundary value problems on each side of the interval using the homogeneous solutions and then fixing the coefficients so that
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)
is continuous at
y=z
and
\partial_{y}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)
has a jump discontinuity at
y=z
. More details can be found in
[
CZN25chan
]
.

4.
Green’s function for weak and strong stratifications

In this section we present the main regularity structures of the Green’s function for
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and we prove Theorem
2.1
through Propositions
4.1
,
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
below.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Then, there exists
\varepsilon_{*}>0
small enough such for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
the Green’s function admits the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}

where
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
and

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W},\,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1},\quad\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W},\,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{H_{k}^{1}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}},

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
and all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

We argue for
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}
since the proof for
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}
and for
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
is identical. For
z<y
, an inspection of the Green’s function and Lemma
B.3
show that

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(1-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}(\eta)

Note that for
z\in I_{3}(y_{0})
we also have

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})

\displaystyle=M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,-\gamma_{0}}(\xi)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad-M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}(\xi)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}},

with
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
. With this we decompose

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}},

Owing to
[
CZN25chan
, Proposition 4.4]
when
y_{0}\in I_{S}
and to Lemma
D.3
when
y_{0}\in I_{W}
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\left|\frac{M_{\tau}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon)}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}M_{\sigma}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{|k|}.

We remark here that for
y_{0}\in I_{S}
, we have that
\nu_{0}
is uniformly bounded from above and from below away from 0, and an inspection of the proof of Proposition 4.4 in
[
CZN25chan
]
shows that the bounds are uniform in
I_{S}
. Together with
\|\mathcal{E}_{0,\pm\gamma_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{10}(0))}\lesssim 1
, we see that

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\sup_{y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\sup_{z\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,\tau}(y,y_{0},z)\right|\lesssim k^{-1},

for all
\sigma,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. To prove the estimates for
\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)
, we note that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})=2k\left(M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}^{\prime}(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}^{\prime}(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\right)

and since

\displaystyle M^{\prime}_{\mathsf{r}}(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\mathcal{E}_{1,\gamma_{0}}(\xi)\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}},

\displaystyle M^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}}(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\mathcal{E}_{1,-\gamma_{0}}(\xi)\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}},

with
\|\mathcal{E}_{1,\pm\gamma_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{10}(0))}\lesssim 1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}}\sup_{y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,\tau}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{k}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim 1.

Next, we address the
H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))
bounds of
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)
. To do so, for
z<y
must obtain
H^{1}_{k}
bounds on
\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})
.
Using the entanglement inequality, if
y_{0}<y
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}^{2}+k^{2}\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}^{2}\lesssim k^{2}\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}^{2},

namely

\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}\lesssim\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}\lesssim\frac{|M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|+|M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}},

(4.1)

where we have used the local bounds of
\phi_{l,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})
when
z\in I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})
. Note further than since
y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, then
(y_{0},y)\cap I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})=\emptyset
and thus
\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y))}=\|\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}
. On the other hand, for
y\leq y_{0}
, we observe that
(0,y)\subseteq(0,y_{0})
and we use the
H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))
bounds of
\phi_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
that we already have.

Summing up, for
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
,
y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
and
z<y
, appealing once again to Proposition 4.2 in
[
CZN25chan
]
we reach

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(0,y))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|+|M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}|M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}

For
y<z
, we consider
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}
, one now needs to study
\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})
. Nevertheless, the bounds and strategies to prove them remain the same as for the case
z<y
, we omit the details. With this, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\sup_{y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}

and the proof is finished.
∎

We need more refined properties for the
\partial_{y}
derivatives of the regular and singular factors of the decomposition.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
. For
z,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, and the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}},

where
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, there holds,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}+k^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\right\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim|\xi|

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
z\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. Moreover, for
f\in C(I_{3}(y_{0}))
,
y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
and
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=f(y)\left(\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}-\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}\right)(y,y_{0},y)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(0,y)\cap I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(y,2)\cap I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

with

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)\right|+\left|\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)\right|\lesssim k|\eta||\xi|

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
, for
\sigma,\,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
.

Proof.

We argue for
\sigma={\mathsf{r}}
. Assume that
z<y
so that
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)={\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)
. Since

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}(\xi)

and
\partial_{z}\xi=2k
, there holds

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)=-2k\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi)

The
X_{k}^{0}
bound on
\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},\cdot)
follow easily as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
once we observe, see Lemma
B.3
, that
\left|\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi)\right|\lesssim|\xi|
.
Regarding the
X_{k}^{1}
bound, note that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}=\begin{cases}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}&\quad z<y,\\
\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}&\quad y<z,\end{cases}

for all
\sigma,\,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. Moreover, an inspection of Proposition
3.1
shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=-4k^{2}\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi)M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,-\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\eta)

since
\partial_{y}\eta=2k
. Therefore, appealing to Proposition
B.3
, we obtain
|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\gamma}^{\prime}(\xi)|\lesssim|\xi|
while
|{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\gamma}^{\prime}(\eta)|\lesssim|\eta|
. With this and the lower bounds on the Wronskian the local estimate for
\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}
follows. The bounds for the other combinations of
\sigma,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
are the same, we omit the details.

We next address the
L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))
estimate. We argue for
\sigma={\mathsf{r}}
and for
z<y
, the other choices follow similarly. Recall form Lemma
B.3
that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)=-2k\frac{1}{{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})}\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi).

with
\left|\mathcal{E}_{0,\gamma_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi)\right|\lesssim|\xi|
, for
|\xi|\leq 4\beta
.
The entanglement inequality in
(y_{0},2)
gives

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(y_{0},2))}^{2}+k^{2}\|\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(y_{0},2))}^{2}\lesssim k^{2}\|\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(y_{0},2))}^{2},

namely

\displaystyle\|\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(y_{0},2))}\lesssim\|\phi_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(y_{0},2))}\lesssim\frac{|M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|+|M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})|}{k^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

On the other hand, since
z\in I_{3}(y_{0})
then
(z,y_{0})\subset I_{3}(y_{0})
and thus
I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})\cap(z,y_{0})=\emptyset
. Appealing once again to the Wrosnkian bounds, confer Proposition 4.2 in
[
CZN25chan
]
, we reach
the desired bounds and the proof is finished.
∎

Exploiting the symmetry of the Green’s function, we obtain the following.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
. For
z,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, and the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}},

where
\eta=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, there holds,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}+k^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim|\eta|

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
.

5.
Green’s function for mild stratifications

Next, we present the main regularity structures of the Green’s function associated to the mild stratification regime, for which a logarithmic correction needs to be incorporated. We assume here that
\tilde{\delta}>0
is small enough so that for all
y_{0}\in I_{M}
we have
|\gamma(y_{0})|\leq\gamma_{*}
, where
\gamma_{*}
is given by Lemma
C.1
,
C.3
and
C.4
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
. There exists
\varepsilon_{*}>0
small enough such that for all
y_{0}\in I_{M}
the Green’s function admits the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)

\displaystyle=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\log(\eta)\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma}(\eta),

where
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma}(\eta)=\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma s\log(\eta)}\mathrm{d}s
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{M},\,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{LX_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1},\quad\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{M},\,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{H_{k}^{1}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
and all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

The arguments are similar to those for Proposition
4.1
. The local bounds are obtained decomposing each homogeneous solution that constitutes the Green’s function into the regularity structures of
M_{\mathsf{r}}
and
W_{\mathsf{r}}
, see Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.4
, respectively. For any of the four possible combinations of pre-factors, namely

\displaystyle M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}),\quad M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}),\quad

\displaystyle W_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}),\quad W_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}),

its quotient with the Wronskian
{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})
is uniformly bounded by
k^{-1}
, confer Lemma
C.1
,
C.3
and
C.4
.

Next, we address the
H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))
bounds of
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)
as in Proposition
4.1
They follow once we now note that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{l,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}^{2}\lesssim|M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}\mp i\varepsilon_{0})|\int_{I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0})}\left(|\xi|^{1+2\mu}+|\xi|^{1-2\mu}|\log(\xi)|^{2}|\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma}(\xi)|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}z,

where
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, with
\beta\leq|\xi|\leq 4\beta
. For
\gamma=\mu+i\nu
, with
\mu,\nu\geq 0
, and
\gamma\in B_{\frac{1}{4}}(0)
, we further observe that for
|\xi|
bounded we have

|Q_{\gamma}(\xi)|\leq\int_{0}^{2}e^{2u\left(\mu\log(|\xi|-\nu\text{Arg}(\xi)\right)}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim\int_{0}^{2}e^{2u\mu\log(|\xi|)}\mathrm{d}u\lesssim 1.

(5.1)

Since
|\log(\xi)|\lesssim 1+|\log(|\xi|)|\lesssim 1+|\log(2k|z-y_{0}|)|
, we then have

\displaystyle\|\phi_{l,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}_{z}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0})\cap(0,y_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}\mp i\varepsilon_{0})|}{k}\int_{2\beta}^{3\beta}\left(x^{1+2\mu}+x^{1-2\mu}(1+|\log^{2}(x)|)\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}\mp i\varepsilon_{0})|}{k}

and the conclusion follows as before.
∎

We next state analogous regularity results to those for the weak and strong stratification regime adapted to the regularity structures of the mild stratification. We omit their proofs, as they are identical to the ones for the weak and strong stratification setting.

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{M}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
. For
z,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, and the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\log(\xi){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma_{0}}(\xi)

where
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, there holds,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}+k^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\right\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim|\xi|

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. Moreover, for
f\in C(I_{3}(y_{0}))
,
y\in I_{3}=I_{3}(y_{0})
and
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=f(y)\left(\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}-\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}\right)(y,y_{0},y)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(0,y)\cap I_{3}}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(y,2)\cap I_{3}}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

with

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)\right|+\left|\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)\right|\lesssim k|\eta||\xi|

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{M}
, for
\sigma,\,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
.

Corollary 5.3
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{M}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
. For
z,y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, and the decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\log(\eta){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma_{0}}(\eta),

where
\eta=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
, there holds,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}+k^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim|\eta|

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
.

6.
Solution operator estimates for the stratified regime

Unless stated otherwise we assume from now on
\varepsilon_{*}>0
is given by Propositions
4.1
and
5.1
. To pave the way for the limiting absorption principle, here we define the error operator

\displaystyle T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y)=\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z){\textsc{E}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})f(z)\mathrm{d}z.

(6.1)

and the solution operators

\displaystyle(R_{m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})=\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{m}}\mathrm{d}z,

(6.2)

for
m=0,1,2,3,4
. The above operators are such that

\textsc{RTG}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y)={\textsc{E}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})f(y)

and

\textsc{RTG}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(R_{m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})=\frac{f(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{m}},

respectively, for
m=0,1,2,3,4
. The purpose of this section is to establish the necessary mapping properties of the operators
T_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
R_{m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
with respect to the spaces
X_{k}
and
LX_{k}
and uniform in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
to apply the limiting absorption principle in Section
10
.

6.1.
Operator estimates for strong and weak stratifications

We first consider the space
X_{k}
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. For
y_{0}\in I_{S}
we recall that
\mu_{0}=0
and
\nu_{0}>0
. Likewise, for
y_{0}\in I_{W}
we have that
\nu_{0}=0
and
\mu_{0}\neq 0
since
\mu_{0}=\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{4}-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}
, with
{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})=\tfrac{{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})^{2}}
. In particular, we record

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=(1-2\mu_{0})\frac{1+2\mu_{0}}{4}v^{\prime}(y_{0})^{2}\lesssim(1-2\mu_{0})

(6.3)

which will prove useful in several estimates below.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
f\in L^{2}({0},{2})
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(0,2)},

uniformly for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
and all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

Let
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=(R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})
, we note that

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\int_{0}^{2}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\int_{0}^{2}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z,

where as usual
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
and we define
\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0})
accordingly. Since
\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\in X_{k}
as a function of
z
uniformly in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and in
y\in I_{3}(y_{0})
, confer Proposition
4.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

where we estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\sup_{y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left|\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\sup_{y\in I_{3}(y_{0})}\left|\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

Similarly, since

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\chi_{I_{3}(y_{0})}(z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{d}z,

an application of Corollary
4.3
shows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\right|\mathrm{d}z\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\eta|\|f\|_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\right|\mathrm{d}z\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\eta|\|f\|_{L^{2}}

Therefore, we see that
\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
uniformly in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. Next, since
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left({\Delta}_{k}-k^{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2}}\right)g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=f(y),

we use the entanglement inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}+\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}

because
\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\|\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-2}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
. With this, the proof is finished.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. Let
f\in L^{\infty}(I_{3})\cap L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})
. There holds

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})},

uniformly both in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
, and in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

Let
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})
, we now have

\displaystyle\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0})=\int_{0}^{2}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z.

Proposition
4.1
yields

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim k^{-1}\frac{\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}}{1-2\mu_{0}},

while there also holds

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim k\left\|\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\right\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}.

On the other hand, from Corollary
4.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z))\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim\frac{\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}}{1-2\mu_{0}}|\eta|,

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}|\eta|.

As a result we get
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})\left\|\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}
. Now we use the equation

\displaystyle\left({\Delta}_{k}-k^{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2}}\right)g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\frac{f(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}

and the entanglement inequality to obtain

\displaystyle k^{2}\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})}\frac{f(y)g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\int_{I_{2}^{c}(y_{0})\cap I_{3}(y_{0}))}\frac{f(y)g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}y\right|+\left|\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}\frac{f(y)g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}y\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}^{0}}+\delta\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}k^{2}|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})|^{2}\mathrm{d}y+C_{\delta}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}^{2}

for some
C_{\delta}>0
. Since
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})\left\|\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}
, for
\delta>0
small enough, there holds

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})^{2}k^{2}\|g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H^{1}_{k}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-2}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}^{2}+C_{\delta}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}^{2}\lesssim k^{-2}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}^{2}+\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})}^{2}

from which we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

In the sequel we shall also need a stronger version of Lemma
6.2
. We record it here.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. Let
f\in Z_{k}
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}

uniformly both in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
, and in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

We remark here that
R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
regularizes from
Z_{k}
to
X_{k}
. This fact will be important later to upgrade the regularity of
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
.

Proof.

Recall that for
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})
we have

\displaystyle\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0})=\int_{0}^{2}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z.

We note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}=\frac{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{-1}}{y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}+\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}-\frac{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{-1}}{y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}

with

\displaystyle\frac{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{-1}}{y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{(y-y_{0})^{2}}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0}+u_{1}u_{2}(y-y_{0}))\mathrm{d}u_{1}\mathrm{d}u_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\in L^{\infty}(0,2)

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in[0,2]
and all
0<\varepsilon
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}\mathrm{d}z-R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\left[\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f\right]

\displaystyle=g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}).

Since
\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\in L^{\infty}(I_{3})
and
f\in Z_{k}
, from Lemma
6.1
we have that

\displaystyle\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}.

We further split

\displaystyle g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=:g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{1,n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

so that from Proposition
4.1
we readily have
\|g_{1,n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}
. On the other hand, using the regularity structures of
f\in Z_{k}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, we have

\displaystyle f(z,y_{0})=f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}

and likewise

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)=\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}

where we recall that
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
. Then,

\displaystyle g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\gamma_{0}}}\mathrm{d}z.

We shall consider
\gamma_{0}=\mu_{0}\neq 0
, since for
\gamma_{0}=i\nu_{0}\neq 0
we can already use Proposition
4.1
to get the desired
X_{k}^{1}
bounds for
g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. Now, for
\gamma_{0}=\mu_{0}\in(\mu_{*},\tfrac{1}{2})
we shall only consider the integral involving the most singular factor
\xi^{-2\mu_{0}}
, namely

\displaystyle 2k

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\mu_{0}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\partial_{z}\left(\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\mu_{0}}-1}{1-2\mu_{0}}\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\left.\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\mu_{0}}-1}{1-2\mu_{0}}\right|_{\partial I_{3}}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\mu_{0}}-1}{1-2\mu_{0}}\mathrm{d}z.

Further observing that

\displaystyle\left|\zeta^{1-2\mu_{0}}-1\right|=(1-2\mu_{0})\left|\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{2}e^{u(1-2\mu_{0})\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}u\right|\lesssim(1-2\mu_{0})|\log(\zeta)|

uniformly in
\mu_{0}\in(\mu_{*},\tfrac{1}{2})
for all
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
|\zeta|
bounded, and the fact that
-\int_{0}^{2}\log(x)\mathrm{d}x
is finite, we obtain from Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
that the solid term is bounded and thus,

\displaystyle\left\|2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\frac{f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\mu_{0}}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{X_{k}^{0}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}^{1}}.

Concerning the
X_{k}^{1}
bound, note that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\partial_{z}\left(f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z.

In particular, Proposition
4.2
applies and

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}\right)_{\tau}-\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}\right)_{\tau}\right)(y,y_{0},y)f_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0})\frac{\eta^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(0,y)\cap I_{3}}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{(y,2)\cap I_{3}}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z.

Further recalling, see Proposition
4.2
, that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{u}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},y)\right|+\left|\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathsf{l}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},y)\right|\lesssim|\eta|,

for
\eta=2k(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
with
|\eta|
bounded, together with

\displaystyle\left|\eta^{1-2\mu_{0}}-1\right|\lesssim(1-2\mu_{0})|\log(\eta)|

uniformly in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
, and Proposition
4.1
we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}\right)_{\tau}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\frac{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim\|f\|_{Z_{k}^{1}}.

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
, for
\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. Thus, the
\left\|\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}
bounds follow from the above, Proposition
4.1
, Corollary
4.3
and the observation that

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z,y_{0})}{z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,{\mathsf{r}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\partial_{y}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+2k\partial_{y}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\sigma,{\mathsf{s}}}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{(2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon))^{2\gamma_{0}}}\mathrm{d}z.

Once the local
X_{k}^{1}
bounds on
g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are obtained, the entanglement inequality provides

\displaystyle\|g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H_{k}^{1}(I_{3}^{c})}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}+k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{2}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}

and the second part of the Lemma follows.
∎

Combining Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.3
we obtain the following result.

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. Let
f\in Z_{k}
and
h\in C^{1}
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}hf)\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}

uniformly both in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
, and in
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

For
h_{1}(y,y_{0})=h(y)-h(y_{0})
we write

\displaystyle R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}hf(y,y_{0})=h(y_{0})R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})+\left(R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\frac{h_{1}(\cdot,y_{0})f(\cdot,y_{0})}{v(\cdot)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\right)(y,y_{0}).

Moreover, since

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{h_{1}(\cdot,y_{0})f(\cdot,y_{0})}{v(\cdot)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{Z_{k}},

the conclusion follows using Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.3
.
∎

The last lemma on
R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
concerns the case where the operator is applied to
H^{1}
functions.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
f\in H^{1}_{k}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H^{1}_{k}}

uniformly for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
and all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

As usual, let
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z-R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\left[V_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})f\right](y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=:g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}).

While Lemma
6.1
gives
\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
, we note that

\displaystyle g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=2k\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z+2k\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{1,n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

so that Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
readily gives
\|g_{1,n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
. As for the local contribution, we have

\displaystyle g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}}{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\right)+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}}{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}-1\right)\right)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}}{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{\xi^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}-1}{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\right)f(z)\Big|_{\partial I_{3}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{1+2\gamma_{0}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\right)\frac{\xi^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}}{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\right)\log(\xi)\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}}(\xi)\mathrm{d}z.

Then,
\|g_{1,\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}
follows from Lemma
2.3
, Propositions
4.1
,
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
and the observations that
\log(\xi)
is squared-integrable, bounded for
|\xi|
bounded uniformly from below and above and
|Q_{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}}(\xi)|
is uniformly bounded for
|\xi|
bounded. The
H_{k}^{1}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))
bounds of
g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
follow from the local
X_{k}^{1}
bounds and the entanglement inequality, we omit the details.
∎

We now consider the most singular operator
R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
that we will need to use the limiting absorption principle. First, we record a basic identity satisfied for functions
f(\cdot,y_{0})\in X_{k}
that will prove useful:

\begin{split}\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},\cdot)f(z,y_{0})\right)&=\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{1+2\gamma_{0}}\\
&\quad+2k(1+2\gamma_{0})\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{2\gamma_{0}}\\
&\quad+\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi\\
&\quad+2k\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\\
&\quad+\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{1-2\gamma_{0}}\\
&\quad+2k(1-2\gamma_{0})\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}},\end{split}

(6.4)

where we recall that
\xi=2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
,
f(y,y_{0})\in Z_{k}
and
h(\cdot,y_{0})\in C^{2}
such that
h(y_{0},y_{0})=0
and
\|h\|_{C^{2}}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
y_{0}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|(R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}hf)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}

(6.5)

uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{*})
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

Let
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}[hf](y,y_{0})
. As usual, we write

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{h(y,y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{h(y,y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=:g_{\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}),

where
\ell
and
n\ell
denote the local and non-local integral contributions, respectively. Since
h(y,y_{0})\in C^{1}
uniformly in
y_{0}
with
h(y_{0},y_{0})=0
we rapidly conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|g_{n\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}

thanks to Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
. On the other hand, integrating by parts,

\displaystyle g_{\ell,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}\Big|_{\partial I_{3}(y_{0})}+\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\frac{{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}).

Since
h\in C^{2}
uniformly in
y_{0}
, we can use Lemma
6.4
to obtain
\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}
. Similarly, since
h(y_{0},y_{0})=0
and
h
is uniformly smooth in
y_{0}
we likewise have, together with Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
, that
\|g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}
. On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)}{2k(z-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=:g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{5,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}).

We shall argue for the
X_{k}
bounds of
g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, since the proof for the
g_{5,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
follows the same lines. From (
6.4
) we get

\displaystyle g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+4k^{2}(1-2\gamma_{0})\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+g_{6,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

From Proposition
4.1
, Corollary
4.3
and the vanishing properties of
h
we have
\|g_{6,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}}
. Moreover, since

\displaystyle\left|h(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{k}|2k|z-y_{0}||^{1-2\mu_{0}}\lesssim\frac{1}{k}+\frac{1}{k}(1-2\mu_{0})|\log(2k|z-y_{0}|)|\in L^{1}(I_{3}(y_{0}))

appealing to Proposition
4.1
, Proposition
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
we again conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|2k\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{X_{k}}.

Finally,

\displaystyle 4k^{2}(1-2\gamma_{0})

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-2k\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2\gamma_{0}}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\Big|_{\partial I_{3}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{0}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\partial_{z}\left(\xi^{1-2\gamma_{0}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+2k\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2\gamma_{0}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{h(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z.

The observation that
\left|h(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\right|\lesssim k^{-1}\left(1+|\log|\xi||\right)
, which is integrable whenever
|\xi|
is bounded and it is bounded whenever
|\xi|
is uniformly bounded from above and below, and the routine application of Propositions
4.1
,
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
yield the desired
X_{k}^{1}
bounds. In turn, the
H^{1}_{k}(I_{3})
estimates follow form the local
X_{k}^{1}
bounds and the entanglement inequality, we omit the details.
∎

6.2.
Operator estimates for mild stratifications

In this subsection we obtain the main estimates on the operators
R_{m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
for
m=0,1,2
when the spectral value ranges in the mildly stratified region. We begin with the analogue of Lemma
6.1
, whose proof is identical.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
f\in L^{2}({0},{2})
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(0,2)},

uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
and in
y_{0}\in I_{M}
.

Since for
y_{0}\in I_{M}
we have that
\mu_{0}\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
, we now obtain a simplified and stronger version of Lemma
6.2
that also covers Lemma
6.3
, Lemma
6.5
in view of Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
6.6
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y_{0}\in I_{M}
. Let
f\in L^{\infty}(I_{3})\cap L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3})}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c})},

uniformly both in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
, and in
y_{0}\in I_{M}
. In particular, if
f=f(\cdot,y_{0})\in LZ_{k}
then

\displaystyle\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{LZ_{k}},

while if
f\in H_{k}^{1}
then there holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}},

uniformly both in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
, and in
y_{0}\in I_{M}
.

Proof.

For
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z-\int_{0}^{2}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}.

We can use Lemma
6.7
to bound
\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}}
. Similarly, Proposition
5.2
, Corollary
5.3
and the entanglement inequality show
\|g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}
. For
g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, we use the regularity structure of
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
to write

\displaystyle g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f(z)\xi^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\log(\xi){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma_{0}}(\xi)\mathrm{d}z.

The lemma then follows once we use Proposition
5.2
, Corollary
5.3
together with the observations that
|{\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma_{0}}(\xi)|
is bounded and
x^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\log(x)
is integrable, with uniform bounds in
|\gamma_{0}|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
.
∎

6.3.
Estimates for the error operator

We are now able to bound the main error term in the reduction of the Taylor-Goldstein operator.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
f\in Z_{k}
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(T_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}},

uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. Similarly, for
y_{0}\in I_{M}
and
f\in LZ_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(T_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{LX_{k}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{LZ_{k}},

uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
and
y_{0}\in I_{M}
.

Proof.

We recall the error operator
{\textsc{E}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and we consider

\displaystyle{\textsc{E}}_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}f(y,y_{0}),

\displaystyle{\textsc{E}}_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)-\mathrm{P}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle{\textsc{E}}_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}-\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2}}\right)f(y,y_{0}).

for which there holds

\displaystyle{\textsc{E}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}=-{\textsc{E}}_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}+{\textsc{E}}_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}+{\textsc{E}}_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}.

For
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=T_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})f(y,y_{0})
and
g_{j,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\sc E_{j,k,\varepsilon}f(y,y_{0})
, we clearly have
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}=-g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}+g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}+g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. We further note that

\displaystyle g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\left(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}v^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)f(\cdot,y_{0})\right)(y),

while

\displaystyle g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\left(R_{2,k,\varepsilon}h(y,y_{0})f(\cdot,y_{0})\right)(y),\quad h(y,y_{0}):=\mathrm{P}(y)-\mathrm{P}(y_{0})

and

\displaystyle g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})={\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})\left(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}\mathrm{V}_{0,\varepsilon}(\cdot,y_{0})f(\cdot,y_{0})\right)(y),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{V}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{0})-v^{\prime}(y_{0})(y-y_{0}))(v(y)-v(y_{0})+v^{\prime}(y_{0})(y-y_{0})\pm 2i\varepsilon)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)(y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2}},

with
\mathrm{V}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\in L^{\infty}_{y}
uniformly in
y_{0}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Since

\displaystyle k^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{0}))}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{Z_{k}},

the
X_{k}
estimates follows once we apply Corollary
6.4
to
g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, Lemma
6.6
to
g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and Lemma
6.2
to
g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. Instead, for the
LX_{k}
estimates we now write

\displaystyle g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\left(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}h(\cdot,y_{0})f(\cdot,y_{0})\right)(y),\quad h(y,y_{0})=\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}_{y,y_{0}}.

and we use Lemma
6.8
for all
g_{j,k\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, with
j=1,2,3
.
∎

7.
The homogeneous Taylor-Goldstein equation

In this section we study solutions to the homogeneous Taylor-Goldstein equation

\left(\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)\varphi=0.

(hTG)

Our arguments are by now standard and follow closely those presented in
[
WZZ18
]
for the 2D Euler equations. Inspired by the Euler index formula, we define

\begin{split}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})&:=(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}),\\
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})&:=(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\end{split}

(7.1)

where we recall that
\gamma_{0}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}
. Then,
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are solutions to (
hTG
) if
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are solutions to

\partial_{y}\left((v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{1+2\gamma_{0}}\partial_{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)-k^{2}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{2\gamma_{0}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}=0

(7.2)

and

\partial_{y}\left((v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{1-2\gamma_{0}}\partial_{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)-k^{2}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{0})^{-2\gamma_{0}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}=0,

(7.3)

respectively. Here, we have defined

\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon-\frac{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}{k^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2k^{2}}v^{\prime\prime}(y)

(7.4)

and

\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon-\frac{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}{k^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{1+2\gamma_{0}}{2k^{2}}v^{\prime\prime}(y).

(7.5)

In order to show the existence of solutions to (
7.2
) and (
7.3
), we define the operators

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\phi(y,y_{0})=\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{1+2\gamma_{0}}}\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2\gamma_{0}}\phi(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

(7.6)

and similarly

{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{s}},1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\phi(y,y_{0})=\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{1-2\gamma_{0}}}\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{-2\gamma_{0}}\phi(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

(7.7)

Moreover, for

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{0}\phi(y,y_{0}):=\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\phi(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

we further define
