\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}(y)=\frac{1}{4}
, then
\widetilde{\varpi}=\tilde{y}
and we use Proposition
8.6
. If there is no such root,
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}(\tilde{(}y))<\frac{1}{4}
and we also use Proposition
8.6
. In all cases we conclude that
|\varphi(0,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})|\geq c_{0}>0
for all
j
large enough, reaching a contradiction with (
8.14
).

\bullet

Case
y_{0}=\widetilde{\varpi}_{1}
and
y_{j}\neq\widetilde{\varpi}_{j,1}
.
In this setting,
\gamma_{j}\neq 0
but
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow 0
so that we cannot use (
8.15
). Instead, we write

\varphi(y,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})=\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(2,y_{j})\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(y,y_{j})-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(y,y_{j})\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(2,y_{j})}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}

(8.16)

where
\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
is given by (
7.10
), which again solves (
8.11
) with
\varphi(2,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})=0
and
\partial_{y}\varphi(2,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})=1
. Now, Proposition
8.9
shows that
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\varphi(0,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})\neq 0
, contradicting again (
8.14
).

\bullet

Case
y_{0}=\widetilde{\varpi}_{1}
and
y_{j}=\widetilde{\varpi}_{j,1}
.
We now have
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})=\frac{1}{4}
for all
j\geq 1
so that
\gamma_{j}=0
already. Hence, we cannot use
\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
as given in (
7.10
) but instead we take

\varphi(y,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})=\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(2,y_{j})\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(y,y_{j})-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(y,y_{j})\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(2,y_{j})}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}

where now
\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j}\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
is given by (
7.14
). Thanks to Proposition
8.11
we obtain
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\varphi(0,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})\neq 0
, contradicting again (
8.14
). This case also covers the setting where
\{y\in[0,2]:\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}(y)=\frac{1}{4}\}
contains an interval.

\bullet

Case
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}
,
\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}=0
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}>0
.
Since
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}\rightarrow 0
, we have that
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}(y)<\frac{1}{4}
for all
y\in[0,2]
and
\gamma_{j}\in(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2})
, for
j\geq 1
large enough. We distinguish according to the limiting value
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}
. As before, let
y_{0},\,y_{j}\in[0,2]
be such that
v(y_{0})=\lambda
and
v(y_{j})={\rm Re}(\lambda_{j})
, respectively.

\bullet

Case
y_{0}=\vartheta_{n}
and
\mathrm{P}(y_{j})=0
.
As before, this case cannot happen due to Lemma
8.3
.

\bullet

Case
y_{0}=\vartheta_{n}
and
\mathrm{P}(y_{j})>0
.
We argue as before, now noting that

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0,0}(y,y_{0})=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}}^{+}(y,y_{j})

(8.17)

satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0,0}(y,y_{0})=1+k^{2}\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\frac{1}{(v(z)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{0}))^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0,0}(s,y_{0})\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z

so that
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(y,y_{0}):=(v(y)-v(y_{0}))\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0,0}(y,y_{0})
satisfies

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}-k^{2}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\right)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(y,y_{0})=0.

Moreover, we have from Proposition
9.4
and Lemma
9.9
that for
\varphi(y,\lambda,0)=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\varphi(y,\lambda_{j},\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{j})
we have

\displaystyle\varphi(y,\lambda,0)=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(y,y_{0})\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(y,y_{0})\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,0}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}z

is well-defined and, moreover, satisfies

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}-k^{2}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\right)\varphi(y,\lambda,0)=0

with
\varphi(2,\lambda,0)=0
and
\partial_{y}\varphi(2,\lambda,0)=1
. In particular, we also have
\varphi(0,\lambda,0)=0
. However, Proposition
9.4
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0,\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}}(y,y_{0})\neq 0
for all
y\in[0,2]\setminus\{y_{0}\}
due to Lemma
8.4
show again that
\varphi(0,\lambda,0)\neq 0
, thus obtaining a contradiction.

\bullet

Case
y_{0}\in(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})
.
We proceed as in the previous case, where now
\varphi(y,\lambda,0)
satisfies

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}-k^{2}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\right)\varphi(y,\lambda,0)=0

together with
\varphi(2,\lambda,0)=0
,
\varphi(0,\lambda,0)=0
and
\partial_{y}\varphi(2,\lambda,0)=1
. Hence,
\lambda\in(v(\vartheta_{1}),v(\vartheta_{2}))
and
\varphi(y,\lambda,{\mathfrak{g}})\neq 0
constitute an eigenvalue and corresponding non-zero eigenfunction of the linearised Euler equations, a contradiction with the spectral assumption.

\bullet

Case
\lambda\in\mathcal{R}
,
\tilde{{\mathfrak{g}}}=\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}=0
.
This setting correspond to having
\lambda_{j}=v(y_{j})
an embedding eigenvalue of the linearised Euler operator, which is in contradiction with H3.
∎

Remark 8.16
.

As one can see from the stability conditions and the proof of Theorem
2.4
, increasing the size of the channel from
[0,2]
to
[0,\mathsf{H}]
for some
\mathsf{H}>2
relaxes the conditions on the background steady state and allows us to find more examples where the region
J(y)\geq\frac{1}{4}
fills most of the channel. In a sense, the absence of physical boundaries may be understood as a stabilising mechanism. For instance, for the stably stratified Couette flow the linearised operator is easily understood in the periodic strip in
[
CZN23strip
]
, while in the periodic channel it presents sequences of neutral eigenvalues and may exhibit unstable eigenvalues, see
[
CZN25chan
]
.

9.
The fragile regime

In this section we study the behaviour of the homogeneous and in-homogeneous solutions of the Taylor-Goldstein equation for
k\geq 1
fixed when the spectral parameter
y_{0}\in I_{W}
is close to the non-stratified region. As
y_{0}\rightarrow\vartheta_{n}
, for
n=1,2
, the
most singular
homogeneous solution
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
degenerates, in that its bounds are not uniform in
y_{0}
and blow up as
y_{0}\rightarrow\vartheta_{n}
. Consequently, we shall use a different homogeneous solution. Once it is established, we use it to construct the solution
h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
to

\textsc{TG}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\mathrm{g}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}),

(9.1)

with
h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0})=h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0})=0
and for some source term
\mathrm{g}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
. The next result is immediate from Proposition 6.5 in
[
WZZ18
]
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
fixed,
\varepsilon>0
and
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
. The solution
h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
to (
9.1
) is given by

\begin{split}h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})&=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}))^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(s,y_{0})\mathrm{g}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(s,y_{0})\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z\\
&\quad+\mathrm{W}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}z\end{split}

(9.2)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{W}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0}):=-\frac{\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}))^{2}}\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{g}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}y}{\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}y}

and
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
is the homogeneous solution to the Taylor-Goldstein operator given by (
7.1
) and Proposition
7.2
.

While
h_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
is well-defined for all
\varepsilon>0
and all
y_{0}\in I_{S}
, here we investigate the solution in the fragile regime, namely when
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
. The main result of the section is the following.

Theorem 9.2
.

Fix
k\geq 1
. Let
y_{0}\in(0,2)
such that
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
. Let
y_{j}\neq y_{0}
, with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})>0
,
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Let
h_{k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
given by (
9.2
) be the solution to (
9.1
), with
\mathrm{g}_{j}(y,y_{j}):=-\textsc{E}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})g_{j}(y)
and
g_{j}\in Z_{k}
with
\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})\|_{Z_{k}}=0.

The theorem follows from Proposition
9.4
, Lemma
9.8
and Lemma
9.9
below.

9.1.
Homogeneous solutions in the fragile regime

We first investigate finer properties of the homogeneous solution
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
, when
y_{0}=\vartheta_{n}
,
n=1,2
, or close to it. Firstly, due to Lemma
8.3
we observe that
\lambda=v(\vartheta_{1})
and
\lambda=v(\vartheta_{2})
are not embedded eigenvalues of
L_{k}
. In particular, for
y_{0}=\vartheta_{n}
with
n=1,2
and

\displaystyle\Psi(y):=-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(y,y_{0})\int_{y}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}

we note that
\Psi(y)
is a non-zero solution to (
8.6
) due to Lemma
8.4
, with further
\Psi(2)=0
,
\Psi^{\prime}(2)=1
, and

\displaystyle\Psi(y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(y,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}(v(2)-v(y))

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(y,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{y}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})^{2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(y,y_{0})\int_{y}^{2}\frac{1}{(v(z)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}z.

Now, since
\lambda=v(\vartheta_{1})
and
\lambda=v(\vartheta_{2})
are not embedded eigenvalues of
L_{k}
we have that
\Psi(0)\neq 0
and thus

\begin{split}0&\neq-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(0,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}(v(2)-v(0))\\
&\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(0,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})^{2}}\mathrm{d}z\\
&\quad-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(2,y_{0})\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(0,y_{0})\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(v(z)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(z,y_{0}))^{2}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}z.\end{split}

(9.3)

Due to Proposition
7.4
, we can extend by continuity the non-vanishing of
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,0}(y,\vartheta_{n})
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Fix
k\geq 1
. Let
y_{0}\in(0,2)
such that
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
. Let
y_{j}\neq y_{0}
, with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})>0
,
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})|\geq\frac{1}{2},

for all
y\in[0,2]
, for
j\geq 0
sufficiently large.

With this uniform lower bound at hand, we now obtain the next result.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
y_{0}\in(0,2)
such that
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
, Let
y_{j}\neq y_{0}
, with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})>0
,
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\frac{v(2)-v(0)}{(v(2)-v(y_{0}))(v(y_{0})-v(0))}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\left(\text{P.V.}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\pm i\pi\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})}{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(y,y_{0}))^{2}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y.

In particular, the limit is non-zero.

Proof.

Firstly, that the limit is non-zero follows from (
9.3
), Lemma
8.4
and
\vartheta_{n}\in(0,2)
, for
n=0,1
. We shall now show the convergence to the claimed limit. Since
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
and
\gamma(y_{0})=\frac{1}{2}
, we have that
\gamma_{j}:=\gamma(y_{j})\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}
. We first write

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\left(\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j}))^{2}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y.

The first contribution is studied separately in Lemma
9.5
below. For the second contribution, to ease notation we denote
\phi_{j}(y):=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})
and
\phi_{0}(y):=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,0}(y,y_{0})
. We further integrate by parts to get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{(\phi_{j}(y,y_{j}))^{2}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y)}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y.

The convergence for the solid boundary term is obvious since the involved functions are continuous in
y_{j}
uniformly a neighbourhood of
y=0
and
y=2
. The convergence of the two integral contributions are studied in Lemma
9.6
and Lemma
9.7
below, where integration by parts arguments are carried out in order to smooth the singularities so that the Dominated Convergence Theorem can be applied. Once the passage to the limit is achieved, the Proposition follows by undoing the integration by parts.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Let
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
, with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. There holds

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\frac{v(2)-v(0)}{(v(2)-v(y_{0}))(v(y_{0})-v(0))}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\left(\text{P.V.}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\pm i\pi\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})}{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{2}}\right)

Proof.

We decompose

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y.

On one hand, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y=-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

and we observe

\displaystyle(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}=\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\left(1+(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1\right)

so that for
y\neq y_{0}
there holds

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}=\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}

because
\left|(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1\right|\lesssim(1-2\gamma_{j})\left|\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\right|
. On the other hand, we claim that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\left(v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{j})\right)\left(\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}-\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}y=0.

Indeed, let
\mathrm{v}(y,y_{j}):=v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{j})-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y_{j})}{(v^{\prime}(y_{j}))^{2}}v^{\prime}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j}))
. We note that
\mathrm{v}(y_{j},y_{j})=\partial_{y}\mathrm{v}(y_{j},y_{j})=0
and that
|\mathrm{v}(y,y_{j})|\lesssim|y-y_{j}|^{2}
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\mathrm{v}(y,y_{j})

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}-\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{v}(y,y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\left((v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y

Since
\left|(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1\right|\lesssim(1-2\gamma_{j})\left|\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\right|
and
\left|\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\right|
is integrable uniformly in
y_{j}
and
\varepsilon_{j}>0
we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{v}(y,y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\left((v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y=0.

Similarly, integrating by parts twice we now see that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{v^{\prime}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j}))}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y-\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=(v(y)-v(y_{j}))\left(\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}-\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\right)\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}-\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}-\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\right)\mathrm{d}y

Further observing that for
\zeta\in C
there holds

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}=\log(\zeta)+(1-2\gamma_{j})\log^{2}(\zeta)\int_{0}^{2}r\int_{0}^{2}e^{(1-2\gamma_{j})rs\log(\xi)}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}r,

since
y_{j}
is bounded away from
0
and
2
uniformly and
v
is monotone, we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{v^{\prime}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y-\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\mathrm{d}y=0

and the claim follows. Therefore,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}\mathrm{d}y.

With this and Lemma 6.3 of
[
WZZ18
]
the proof is concluded.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

In the setting of Proposition
9.4
, there holds

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)\frac{\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y=\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)\frac{\frac{1}{\phi_{0}^{2}(y)}-1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\mathrm{d}y

Proof.

We integrate by parts again:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)

\displaystyle(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\right)\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

As before, the solid boundary term converges, while now for the integral term we recall that

\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}=\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})+(1-2\gamma_{j})\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(\xi),

(9.4)

for
\xi=v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}
and with
\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}(\xi)\right|\lesssim 1
. Then, since
\left|1-\phi_{j}^{2}(y)\right|\lesssim|y-y_{j}|\lesssim|v(y)-v(y_{j})|
, the dominated convergence theorem shows that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{0}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\log(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}-1\right)\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-2(1-2\gamma_{j})\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(\xi)\mathrm{d}y

and we readily observe that the second term vanishes. For the first one, since

\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)={\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}\phi_{j}(y)=\frac{1}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z

(9.5)

we integrate by parts again to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\frac{\int_{y_{0}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z,y_{0})(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}\phi_{j}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y,y_{0})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\right)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}){\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}\phi_{j}(y)\mathrm{d}y

Since
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}\phi_{j}\|_{Y}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
j\geq 1
, we readily obtain convergence of the first and third integral using the Dominated Convergence Theorem. As for the second integral, we now exploit the structure of
\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y,y_{0})
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y,y_{0})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2k^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})(v^{\prime}(y)^{2}-v^{\prime}(y_{j})^{2})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1-2\gamma_{j}}{4k^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}v^{\prime\prime}(y)\mathrm{d}y.

Clearly, the third term vanishes and we can use the Dominated Convergence Theorem on the first integral. For the second integral we shall integrate by parts once again and reach

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})(v^{\prime}(y)^{2}-v^{\prime}(y_{j})^{2})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\frac{\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{3}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})(v^{\prime}(y)^{2}-v^{\prime}(y_{j})^{2})}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{6}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{3}\right)\left({\mathcal{P}}(y)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})(v^{\prime}(y)^{2}-v^{\prime}(y_{j})^{2})\right)\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{6}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{3}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}(y)-2{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})v^{\prime}(y)v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{2}(y)}\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y.

Further observing that
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{J}}
are
C^{1}
functions with
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}(y_{0})={\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})=0
, we can take the limits in all the above integrals using the Dominated Convergence Theorem. Integrating by parts backwards we reach the claim.
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

In the setting of Proposition
9.4
, there holds

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y=2\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{0}^{3}(y)}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}.

Proof.

Firstly, we split

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}-1\right)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y.

For the first integral, we use the Dominated Convergence Theorem, since
\|\phi_{j}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
j\geq 1
,
|\phi_{j}|
is bounded from below and from above uniformly in
j\geq 1
, with
|\phi_{j}(y)-1|\lesssim|y-y_{j}|
and

\displaystyle|y-y_{j}|^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\lesssim 2^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\lesssim 1

since
\zeta^{1-2\gamma_{j}}
is increasing in
\zeta
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{3}(y)}-1\right)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y=\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\prime}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{\frac{1}{\phi_{0}^{3}(y)}-1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\mathrm{d}y.

For the second integral, using (
9.5
) and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}

\displaystyle(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z,y_{j})(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{4\gamma_{j}}}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{y}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z,y_{j})(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{4\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{4\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y,y_{j})\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y.

The convergence of the boundary term is immediate, while that of the first integral follows from the Dominated Convergence Theorem. Thus, we focus on the second integral, which we can further write as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{F}_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y,y_{j})\phi_{j}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(y)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\phi_{j}(y)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1-2\gamma_{j}}{k^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y.

The first contribution converges thanks to the Dominated Convergence Theorem, while the third contribution vanishes in the limit. Indeed,

\displaystyle\frac{1-2\gamma_{j}}{k^{2}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)\phi_{j}(y)}{k^{2}(v^{\prime}(y))^{3}}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)\phi_{j}(y)}{k^{2}(v^{\prime}(y))^{3}}\right)^{\prime}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}y

and we note that
(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\rightarrow 1
weakly in
L^{1}_{y}
and pointwise for all
y\neq y_{0}
. Concerning the second contribution, we integrate by parts to find

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\phi_{j}(y)\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}^{2\gamma_{j}}}\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y)-{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}\frac{{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y.

The convergence of the first two terms being obvious (using the Dominated Convergence Theorem for the integral), we focus on the third term, for which we integrate by parts again. Appealing to (
9.4
), we see that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2}\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y)}

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{j}(y){\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\frac{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{y=0}^{y=2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}(y){\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\right)\log(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-(1-2\gamma_{j})\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{\phi_{j}(y){\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{2,\gamma_{j}}(\xi_{j})\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}y,

where we denote
\xi_{j}=v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}
. The convergence of the first term is immediate, while the third term vanishes in the limit. To prove the convergence of the second term we argue as in the proof of Lemma
9.6
, we omit the routine details.
∎

9.2.
In-homogeneous solutions in the fragile regime

Next, we address the convergence of
h_{k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
. To ease notation, we denote

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y):=(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(y)

where we recall that
\phi_{j}(y)=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
.

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}
with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0^{+}
for
j\rightarrow\infty
. Let
\mathrm{g}_{j}:=-\textsc{E}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}g_{j}
and
g_{j}\in Z_{k}
with
\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\int_{y_{j}}^{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(s)\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k}}=0,

In particular,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\int_{y_{j}}^{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(s)\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z=0

since
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)
is uniformly bounded away from 0.

Proof.

We decompose

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{y_{j}}^{z}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(s)\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\left(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}(s)-1\right)\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}^{2}(z)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{3}h_{n,j}(y)

We next recall that
\mathrm{g}_{j}:=-\textsc{E}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}g_{j}
and
g_{j}\in Z_{k}
with
\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, we have from (
2.8
) that

\displaystyle\left|\textsc{E}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z)\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{|v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}|}

and with (
10.5
) and (
10.6
) we conclude that

\left|\mathrm{g}_{j}(y)\right|\lesssim|v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}.

(9.6)

for all
y\in(0,2)
, since now
k\geq 1
is fixed. Hence, thanks to Lemma
9.3
, we deduce

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\left(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}(s)-1\right)\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

and thus
\left\|h_{1,j}(y)\right\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}^{2}(z)}-1\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

so that
\left\|h_{2,j}\right\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. For the last term, we integrate by parts and obtain

\displaystyle h_{3,j}(y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=h_{4,j}(y)+h_{5,j}(y)+h_{6,j}(y)+h_{7,j}(y)

We further note that

\displaystyle h_{4,j}(y)=\left(\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y)}\int_{y_{j}}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)(v(y)-v(y)\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}

and since

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y)}\int_{y_{j}}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{j}))\cap\dot{H}^{1}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

we deduce that
\left\|h_{4,j}(y)\right\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. Likewise,

\displaystyle\left\|-\frac{\phi_{j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(0)}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{0}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(0)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{j}))\cap\dot{H}^{1}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

and thus
\|h_{5,j}\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. Next, regarding
h_{6,j}
we note that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{g}_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

from which we deduce that
\|h_{6,j}\|\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. Lastly, since

\displaystyle\mathrm{g}_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}g_{j}(y)+\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)-\mathrm{P}(y_{j})}{(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}g_{j}(y)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}(y_{j})}{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}-\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{2}}\right)g_{j}(y)

we further decompose
h_{7,j}(y)=\sum_{n=8}^{10}h_{n,j}(y)
. Firstly,

\displaystyle h_{8,j}(y)

\displaystyle=-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{g_{j}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}(g_{j})_{{\mathsf{r}}}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=h_{11,j}(y)+h_{12,j}(y).

Using (
10.6
) we see that
\|h_{11,j}\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. On the other hand, for
h_{12,j}
we integrate by parts once,

\displaystyle h_{12,j}(y)

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\left(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)\frac{\xi^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\left(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(r_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(\xi)\log(\xi)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=h_{13,j}(y)+h_{14,j}(y),

where
\xi:=v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}
. We now write

\displaystyle h_{13,j}(y)

\displaystyle=\left(h_{13,j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}},

\displaystyle\left(h_{13,j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y)

\displaystyle:=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{(v^{\prime}(y))^{2}}\left(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(y)(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\frac{\eta^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}

with
\eta=v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}
and

\displaystyle\|\left(h_{13,j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}\|_{L^{\infty}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}+\|\partial_{y}\left(h_{13,j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y)\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

because
\|\zeta^{2\gamma_{j}-1}\log(\zeta)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim 1
uniformly for
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}
. Hence,
\|h_{13,j}\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. Arguing as before,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\left(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(r_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}(\xi)\log(\xi)\right\|_{L^{2}_{z}(0,2)}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}

and thus
\|h_{14,j}\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. This completes the
Z_{k}
estimate of
h_{8,j}
. For
h_{9,j}(y)
, we indicate how to obtain analogous bounds. We write

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{3}{2}+\gamma_{j}}}\frac{g_{j}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

and as before we easily observe that the first integral is bounded in
Z_{k}
by
\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k}}
. For the second integral, we integrate by parts once more

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}\partial_{z}\left(\left(\frac{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}}\right)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)-{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z.

We argue as in
h_{4,j}
for the solid boundary term. For the first integral term we argue as for
h_{12,j}
while for the second integral term we argue as for
h_{6,j}
, we omit the routine details. Finally, for
h_{10,j}
we first write

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{{\mathcal{J}}(y_{j})}{(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\frac{1}{(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}

Hence,

\displaystyle h_{10,j}(y)

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}}\frac{1}{(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{3}{2}-\gamma_{j}}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}}\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{3}{2}+\gamma_{j}}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{r}}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\left(b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{3}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{v^{\prime}(z)}

\displaystyle\quad+\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}\left(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\frac{(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1+2\gamma_{j}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{v^{\prime}(z)},

where
b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
and
B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
are defined in (
10.5
). Further observing that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right|+\left|\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}\right|\lesssim|z-y_{j}|

and

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}\right)\right|+\left|\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v(z)-v(y_{j})-v^{\prime}(y_{j})(z-y_{j})}{z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}}\right)\right|\lesssim 1

uniformly in
z,y_{j}\in(0,2)
and
\varepsilon_{j}>0
, we see that the first two integrals can be bounded as for
h_{6,j}
, while for the last two integrals we argue as for
h_{9,j}
, we omit the details.
∎

Once the next lemma is established, the proof of Theorem
9.2
is complete.

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}\in(0,2)
such that
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{j})>0
and
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim 1.

Proof.

The arguments follow closely those of Proposition
9.4
and Lemma
9.8
. Integrating by parts,

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{1}{(\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z))^{2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(z)}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}^{-2}(z)\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\phi_{j}^{-2}(z)\frac{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)\phi_{j}^{3}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\int_{0}^{y}\partial_{z}\left(\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\frac{1}{\phi_{j}^{2}(z)}\right)\frac{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}-1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathrm{d}z

so that we argue as in
h_{4,j}
for the first boundary term and as in
h_{13,j}
for the second boundary term, while we address the second integral as in
h_{14,j}
. For the first integral, since
\phi_{j}^{\prime}(y)={\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathsf{r}},1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}\phi_{j}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{\gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\frac{\phi_{j}^{\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)\phi_{j}^{3}(z)}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}^{2}}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}F_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}(s,y_{j})(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}\phi_{j}^{3}(z)}\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}^{2}}\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\int_{y_{j}}^{z}F_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}(s,y_{j})(v(s)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{j}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{(v^{\prime}(z))^{2}\phi_{j}^{3}(z)}\right)\mathrm{d}z

And thus we argue as in
h_{4,j}
for the solid term, and as in
h_{6,j}
for the integral contribution.
∎

10.
The limiting absorption principle for the stratified regime

We are now in position to establish coercive bounds via the limiting absorption principle in the
Z_{k}
space.

Proposition 10.1
.

There exists some
\kappa>0
such that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Z_{k}}\leq\kappa\|f+T_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f\|_{Z_{k}},

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

Assume towards a contradiction that we can find a sequence of parameters
k_{j}\geq 1
,
y_{j}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
such that
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{*}
,
\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0^{+}
and
f_{j}\in Z_{k_{j}}
, with
\|f_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{j}}}=1
such that

\displaystyle\|f_{j}+T_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}f_{j}(\cdot,y_{j})\|_{Z_{k_{j}}}\rightarrow 0

(10.1)

as
j\rightarrow\infty
. From Proposition
6.9
,
X_{k}\subset Z_{k}
and (
10.1
), we deduce that
|k_{j}|\lesssim 1
and hence
k_{j}\rightarrow k_{*}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
up to a subsequence. In particular,
k_{j}\equiv k_{*}
for all
j
sufficiently large. In what follows, we already consider
j
large enough so that
k_{j}=k_{*}
. Now, let
g_{j}(y):=f_{j}(y)+T_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}f_{j}(y,y_{j})
and define
h_{j}(y)=f_{j}(y)-g_{j}(y)
. It is such that
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}=1
and

\displaystyle h_{j}(y)+T_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}h_{j}(y,y_{j})=-T_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}g_{j}(y,y_{j})

(10.2)

In particular, we now have that
h_{j}(0)=h_{j}(2)=0
. Next, applying
\text{RTG}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
to both sides we obtain

\textsc{TG}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}h_{j}(y,y_{j})=-\textsc{E}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}g_{j}(y,y_{j}).

(10.3)

To solve the equation, we distinguish whether the limiting spectral parameter
y_{0}
is such that
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
or not.

\bullet

Case
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})\neq 0
.
Then
\gamma_{0}\neq\frac{1}{2}
and since
h_{j}(0)=h_{j}(2)=0
, we have that

\displaystyle h_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)}\right)\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y_{j})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\left(\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)

where here
\phi_{\sigma,j}(y)=\phi_{\sigma,k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})
, for
\sigma={\mathsf{s}},\,{\mathsf{r}}
, denote the pair of linearly independent homogeneous solutions of the
\textsc{TG}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
operator well defined in Proposition
7.2
because
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow\gamma_{0}\neq\frac{1}{2}
and
\mathrm{g}_{j}:=-\textsc{E}_{k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}g_{j}
. Since
k_{*}
is fixed, for
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
we have from Proposition
8.7
if
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}\in I_{S}
and Proposition
8.6
if
y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}\in I_{W}
that

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\neq 0.

(10.4)

Next, for

b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}):=\frac{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}{y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}},\quad B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}):=\frac{1}{b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})}

(10.5)

a direct computations shows that

\|b_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|B_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}b_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}B_{\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\lesssim 1,

(10.6)

uniformly for all
y_{j}\in(0,2)
and all
\varepsilon_{j}>0
. Hence,
since
|\textsc{E}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})|\lesssim|v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}|^{-1}
and
k_{*}
is fixed, we deduce that
\|\phi_{\sigma,j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}\lesssim 1
for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
and

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\textsc{E}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\left(g_{j}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}.

We next claim that

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\textsc{E}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\left(g_{j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}},

for which we note that

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\textsc{E}_{1,k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})(z-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\left(g_{j}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{-2\gamma_{j}}v^{\prime\prime}(z)(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\Big|_{z=0}^{z=y}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\partial_{z}\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\mathrm{d}z

where
\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(z,y_{j}):=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}(B_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(z,y_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)(g_{j})_{\mathsf{s}}(z)
and
\|\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{j})\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{y}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}
, uniformly in
y_{j}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
\varepsilon_{j}>0
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|1-2\gamma_{j}|}\left\|\int_{0}^{y}(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\partial_{z}\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(z,y_{j})\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{y}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-2\gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{1-2\gamma_{j}}\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}(y)\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})\right)(v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}

with now
\left\|\frac{v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}}{1-2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},1,j}(y)\mathsf{g}_{j}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{y}(I_{3}(y_{j}))}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}
, since
1-2\gamma_{j}
is uniformly bounded away from 0 because
\gamma_{0}\neq\frac{1}{2}
. The estimates for
\textsc{E}_{2,k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
and
\textsc{E}_{3,k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}
are deduced similarly, integrating by parts to soften out the potential singularities in the spirit of Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
6.6
, we omit the routine details. Finally, the above arguments also provide

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{\sigma,j}(y)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{\tau,j}(z)g_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}

for all
\sigma,\tau\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
. As a result, this shows that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}\lesssim\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|g_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}=0,

a contradiction with
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{Z_{k_{*}}}=1
.

\bullet

Case
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})=0
.
Now
\gamma_{0}=\frac{1}{2}
and we can no longer use Proposition
7.2
to find the solution to (
10.3
) by means of
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}
and
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}
, since
\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}
is a priori not well-defined. Instead, we appeal to Proposition
9.1
and Theorem
9.2
to conclude that
h_{j}(y)\rightarrow 0
for all
y\in(0,2)
, thus reaching a contradiction with
\|h_{j}\|_{X_{k}}=1
.
∎

Proposition 10.2
.

There exists some
\kappa>0
such that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{LZ_{k}}\leq\kappa\|f+T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}f\|_{LZ_{k}},

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{M}
and
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

Arguing as in Proposition
10.1
, we assume towards a contradiction that there exist
k\geq 1
,
y_{j}\in I_{M}
,
\varepsilon_{j}>0
,
h_{j}\in LX_{k}
and
r_{j}\in LX_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\textsc{TG}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}h_{j}(y,y_{j})=-\textsc{E}_{k_{*},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})g_{j}(y,y_{j})

with
h_{j}(0,y_{j})=h_{j}(2,y_{j})=0
and

\displaystyle y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}\in I_{M},\quad\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0,\quad\|h_{j}\|_{LX_{k}}\rightarrow 1,\quad\|g_{j}\|_{LX_{k}}\rightarrow 0,

as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Just as before, we distinguish two cases according to the limiting
y_{0}\in I_{M}
.

\bullet

Case
{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})\neq\frac{1}{4}
.
Then
\gamma_{0}\neq\frac{1}{2}
and also
\gamma_{0}\neq 0
, so that we can write

\displaystyle h_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)}\right)\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y_{j})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\left(\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)

as before, where
\phi_{\sigma,j}(y)=\phi_{\sigma,k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})
, for
\sigma={\mathsf{s}},\,{\mathsf{r}}
are defined as in (
7.1
). In particular, since
\gamma_{0}\neq 0
we see from Proposition
8.7
for
\gamma_{0}\in i{\mathbb{R}}
and Proposition
8.6
for
\gamma_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\neq 0.

Furthermore, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}

since
\zeta^{-2\gamma_{j}}\log(\zeta)\in L^{1}_{loc}
uniformly for all
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow\gamma_{0}\neq\frac{1}{2}
, while we now write

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)}{2\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}

with further

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle=(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\frac{(b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})-(b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})}{2\gamma_{j}}

\displaystyle\quad+(b_{\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j}))^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},k,1,k,\varepsilon_{j}}(y,y_{j})(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j})^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\log(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}){\mathcal{Q}}_{1,\gamma_{j}}(y-y_{j}\pm i\varepsilon_{0,j}).

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{2\gamma_{j}}\right\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim 1.

for
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow\gamma_{0}\neq 0
. Moreover, since

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\sigma,j}\mathrm{g}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(0,2)}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}

for
\sigma\in\{{\mathsf{r}},{\mathsf{s}}\}
, we also deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{2\gamma_{j}}\left(\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\right\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}

and thus we obtain
\|h_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
, contradicting
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}=1
.

\bullet

Case
{\mathcal{J}}(y_{0})=\frac{1}{4}
.
Now we have
\gamma_{0}=0
, for which we use the homogeneous solutions
\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y):=\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
and
\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y):=\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},k,\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}(y,y_{j})
given by (
7.10
) and whose Wronskian is
v^{\prime}(y_{j})
, to construct

\displaystyle h_{j}(y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z}{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(0)}\right)\frac{\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{j})}\left(\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right).

Now, Proposition
8.9
shows that

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(2)-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(0)\neq 0.

Further recalling that

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y)=\eta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{j}}\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},1,j}(y)+\eta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{j}}\log(\eta){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma_{j}}(\eta)\phi_{{\mathsf{s}},j,1}(y)

with
\eta=v(y)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j}
, we readily see that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y)-\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(0)\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim 1

and

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(2)\int_{0}^{2}\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}

as
\zeta^{-2\gamma_{j}}\log^{2}(\zeta)\in L^{1}
uniformly for
\gamma_{j}\rightarrow\gamma_{0}=0
. Hence, we also have that
\|\phi_{\sigma,j}\mathrm{g}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(0,2)}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}
and thus

\displaystyle\left\|\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z-\phi_{{\mathsf{r}},j}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\phi_{{\mathrm{L}},j}(z)\mathrm{g}_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}},

from which we deduce that
\|h_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}\lesssim\|g_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}
, reaching a contradiction again with
\|h_{j}\|_{LZ_{k}}\rightarrow 1
as
j\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

Remark 10.3

(Spectral conditions)
.

For the limiting absorption principle to hold, an inspection of the proof shows that Lemma
8.5
, Lemma
9.3
, and Proposition
9.4
are key to obtain Proposition
8.6
and Theorem
9.2
, respectively. Thus, the coercive estimate in Propositions
10.1
and
10.2
hold for all background stably stratified monotone shear flows for which Lemma
8.5
, Lemma
9.3
, and Proposition
9.4
remain true, which essentially preclude the existence of generalized embedded eigenvalues.

11.
The non-stratified regime

This section aims at proving estimates analogue to those of Propositions
10.1
and
10.2
for
y_{0}\in I_{E}
, the non-stratified regime. Now, for
y_{0}\in I_{E}
the Taylor-Goldstein equation is not as singular and we can use the ideas of
[
Jia20
,
JiaGev20
]
. For
k\geq 1
, let
G_{k}(y,z)
denote the Green’s function of the operator
\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}
with homogeneous boundary conditions at
y=0
and
y=2
. It is given explicitly by

G_{k}(y,z)=-\frac{1}{k\sinh(k)}\begin{cases}\sinh(k(2-z))\sinh(ky),&\text{for }y\leq z,\\
\sinh(kz)\sinh(k(2-y)),&\text{for }z\leq y\end{cases}

(11.1)

and it is such that

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[0,2]}\|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}G_{k}(y,z)\|_{L^{2}_{z}(0,2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}+\alpha}

(11.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in[0,2]}\|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\log(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)\|_{L^{1}_{z}(0,2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-2+\alpha}\log(k)

(11.3)

for
\alpha=0,1
,
y_{0}\in[0,2]
and
0\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
. For each
f\in L^{2}(0,2)
we define

T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0}):=\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\left(-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)f(z)\mathrm{d}z

(11.4)

and

R_{E,m,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0}):=\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{f(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{m}}\mathrm{d}z

(11.5)

for
m=0,1
. Therefore, we can rewrite (
2.16
) as

\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=R_{E,1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\omega_{k}^{0}(y,y_{0})+R_{E,0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varrho_{k}^{0}(y,y_{0})

(11.6)

with
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(0,y_{0})=\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(2,y_{0})=0
.

11.1.
Operator estimates for the non-stratified regime

To capture the precise regularity in the wave-number
k\geq 1
, we recall the space

\|g\|_{H_{k}^{1}}:=\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,2)}+k^{-1}\|g^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,2)}.

The next result extends
[
JiaGev20
, Lemma 3.1]
to the operator
T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{E}
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. There holds

\|T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}\lesssim|k|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}

(11.7)

for all
f\in H_{k}^{1}
. Furthermore, we also have

\left\|\partial_{y}T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)}f(y)\log(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)\right\|_{W_{y}^{1,1}(0,2)}\lesssim k^{\frac{4}{3}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}},

(11.8)

for all
f\in H_{k}^{1}

Proof.

For the first part of the lemma, thanks to
[
JiaGev20
, Lemma 3.1]
, we shall only prove

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{H_{k}^{1}}\lesssim|k|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}.

The estimate (
11.2
) together with
{\mathcal{P}}(y_{0})={\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}(y_{0})=0
and
{\mathcal{P}}\in C^{2}
, and the usual Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives the desired bound. The second statement follows similarly, appealing to
[
JiaGev20
, Lemma 3.1]
we just need to show that

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{y}\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{W_{y}^{1,1}(0,2)}\lesssim|k|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}.

Now, (
11.2
) directly gives

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{y}\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{L^{1}_{y}(0,2)}\lesssim|k|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}},

while for the
\dot{W}^{1,1}
estimate, we note that
\partial_{y}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)=k^{2}g_{k}(y,z)+\delta(y-z)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial^{2}_{y}\int_{0}^{2}G_{k}(y,z)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{L^{1}_{y}(0,2)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}f(y)\right\|_{L^{1}_{y}(0,2)}

\displaystyle\quad+k^{2}\left\|\int_{0}^{2}\frac{G_{k}(y,z){\mathcal{P}}(z)f(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{L^{1}_{y}(0,2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}

and the lemma follows.
∎

The following
H_{k}^{1}
bound on
R_{E,0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
is immediate from (
11.2
) as well.

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{E}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\|R_{E,0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})\|_{H_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(0,2)},

for all
f\in L^{2}(0,2)
.

Similarly, we have the following.

Lemma 11.3

(Lemma 3.1 in
[
JiaGev20
]
)
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{E}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\|R_{E,1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f(y,y_{0})\|_{H_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|f\|_{H_{k}^{1}}

for all
f\in H_{k}^{1}
.

11.2.
The limiting absorption principle in the non-stratified regime

Next, we show the main limiting absorption principle coercive bound:

Proposition 11.4
.

There exists
\kappa>0
such that

\|f\|_{H^{1}_{k}}\leq\kappa\|f+(T_{E,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{H^{1}_{k}}

(11.9)

for all
y_{0}\in I_{E}
,
k\geq 1
,
\varepsilon>0
and
f\in H_{k}^{1}(0,2)
.

Proof.

We argue as in the proof of
[
JiaGev20
, Lemma 3.2]
: by contradiction, assume there exist sequences
k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
,
y_{j}\in I_{E}
,
\varepsilon_{j}>0
and functions
f_{j}\in H_{k_{j}}^{1}
with
\|f_{j}\|_{H_{k_{j}}^{1}(0,2)}=1
, for all
j\geq 1
, such that

k_{j}\rightarrow k_{0}\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})\cup\{\pm\infty\},\quad y_{j}\rightarrow y_{0}\in I_{E},\quad\varepsilon_{j}\rightarrow 0

and

\|f_{j}+T_{E,k_{j},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}f_{j}(\cdot,y_{j})\|_{H_{k_{j}}^{1}}\rightarrow 0

(11.10)

as
j\rightarrow\infty
. Thanks to (
11.7
) and (
11.10
), it is clear that
|k_{j}|\lesssim 1
, and thus
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus\{0\}
. Since
\|g_{j}\|_{H_{k_{j}}^{1}}=1
, the embedding
W^{1,1}(0,2)\rightarrow L^{2}(0,2)
is compact and we have the estimate (
11.8
), we deduce that
T_{E,k_{0},\varepsilon_{j}}f(\cdot,y_{j})
converges in
H^{1}_{k_{0}}
up to subsequences. Hence, from (
11.10
) we see that
f_{j}\rightarrow f
in
H^{1}_{k_{0}}
, with
\|f\|_{H^{1}_{k_{0}}}=1
. Then, since

\displaystyle T_{E,k_{0},\varepsilon_{j}}^{\pm}f_{j}(y,y_{j})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2}\partial_{z}\left(G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}f_{j}(z)\right)\log(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{2}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{j})\pm i\varepsilon_{j})^{2}}f_{j}(z)\mathrm{d}z

and for
\alpha=0,1
uniformly in
y_{j}\in I_{E}
and
\varepsilon_{j}>0
we see that

f(y)-\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{2}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(z)}{v(z)-v(y_{j})+i\varepsilon_{j}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{2}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\frac{P(z)}{(v(z)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}f(z)\mathrm{d}z=0

almost everywhere in
[0,2]
. Then, applying
\partial_{y}^{2}-k^{2}
we reach

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}f(y)-k^{2}f(y)-\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}(v(y)-v(y_{j}))\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)f(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{j}))^{2}+\varepsilon_{j}^{2}}

\displaystyle+iC_{y_{0}}v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})f(y_{0})\delta(y-y_{0})

\displaystyle+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}f(y)=0

in the sense of distributions for
y\in(0,2)
. Multiplying by
\overline{f(y)}
, integrating by parts over
(0,2)
and taking the imaginary part we reach

\displaystyle 0=v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})f(y_{0})

with also
v^{\prime\prime}f\in H_{k_{0}}^{1}
. Therefore, since
\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)f(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\in L^{2}
and
\frac{P(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\in L^{\infty}
, we have

\mathrm{H}(y):=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}f(y)-\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}f(y)\in L^{2}_{y}(0,2)

(11.11)

and
\Delta_{k}f=\mathrm{H}
. Moreover, we also define

\Upsilon(y):=\frac{\mathcal{P}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}f(y)\in L^{2}_{y}(0,2)

(11.12)

and we further note that

\begin{split}(v(y)-v(y_{0}))\mathrm{H}(y)-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\int_{0}^{2}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\mathrm{H}(z)\mathrm{d}z+\Upsilon(y)&=0,\\
(v(y)-v(y_{0}))\Upsilon(y)-\mathcal{P}(y)\int_{0}^{2}G_{k_{0}}(y,z)\mathrm{H}(z)\mathrm{d}z&=0.\end{split}

(11.13)

This shows that the pair
(\mathrm{H},\Upsilon)
is an
L^{2}
eigenfunction of the linearised operator, with embedded eigenvalue
v(y_{0})
, a contradiction with Proposition
8.2
and Lemma
8.3
.
∎

As in the limiting absorption principle for
Z_{k}
and
LZ_{k}
, the coercive estimate of Proposition
11.4
holds if the linearised operator
L_{k}
does not have embedding eigenvalues in the non-stratified region.

11.3.
Sobolev regularity in the non-stratified regime

With Proposition
11.4
at hand, Lemma
11.2
, Lemma
11.3
and (
11.6
) yield

Proposition 11.5
.

Let
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
solve (
11.6
). Then,

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{H_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|w_{k}^{0}\|_{H_{k}^{1}}+k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|q_{k}^{0}\|_{L^{2}}

for all
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{E}
and
\varepsilon>0
.

Since the initial data
\omega_{k}^{0}
and
\varrho_{k}^{0}
is supported in the stratified regime, we next show that the spectral density function in the non-stratified regime vanishes as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Then,

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})=0,

for all
y_{0}\in I_{E}
and all
y\in[0,2]
.

Proof.

Since
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-\varrho_{k}^{0}(y)
, we have that

\displaystyle\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(y,y_{0}):=\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})=\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})

and it is straightforward to see that
\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(y,y_{0})
satisfies

\displaystyle\text{TG}_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2i\varepsilon}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}}w_{k}^{0}(y)+\frac{2i\varepsilon v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle-{\mathcal{P}}(y)\frac{2i\varepsilon(v(y)-v(y_{0}))}{((v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}+\varepsilon^{2})^{2}}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0}).

Observing that
({\text{supp }}\omega_{k}^{0}\cup{\text{supp }}v^{\prime\prime})\cap I_{E}=\emptyset
and
{\mathcal{P}}\equiv 0
in
I_{E}
with
{\mathcal{P}}\in C^{2}
we deduce that

\displaystyle\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0}):=\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(y,y_{0})\in L^{2}(0,2)

satisfies

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{k}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0})-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0})+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0})=0

in the sense of distributions. As before, setting

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0})-\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0}))^{2}}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0}),

\displaystyle\Upsilon(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})}\psi_{k,0}(y,y_{0}),

we see that
\mathrm{H}(\cdot,y_{0}),\Upsilon(\cdot,y_{0})\in L^{2}_{y}(0,2)
and they satisfy (
11.13
). Hence,
v(y_{0})
is an embedded eigenvalue, a contradiction with Proposition
8.2
and Lemma
8.3
.
∎

12.
Sobolev regularity of the spectral density function

With the limiting absorption principle coercive estimates at hand we now show Sobolev regularity of the solutions
{\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
to

\left({\Delta}_{k}-\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+\frac{{\mathcal{P}}(y)}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\right){\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\frac{w_{k}^{0}(y)}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}+q_{k}^{0}(y).

(12.1)

To keep track of the regularity of the initial data, for
k\geq 1
and
j\geq 0
we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{k,j}=k^{j}\left(\|w_{k}^{0}\|_{H_{k}^{j+1}}+k^{-1}\|q_{k}^{0}\|_{H_{k}^{j}}\right),

(12.2)

where we further define

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H_{k}^{j}}:=\sum_{n=0}^{j}k^{-n}\|\partial_{y}^{n}f\|_{L^{2}}

for all
f\in H^{j}
. In particular, we note that
k{\mathcal{S}}_{k,j}\leq{\mathcal{S}}_{k,j+1}
.

Proposition 12.1
.

Let
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
{\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
a solution to (
12.1
). Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\|{\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}.

uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

Note that

\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\left(R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\omega_{k}^{0}\right)(y,y_{0})+\left(R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varrho_{k}^{0}\right)(y,y_{0})

(12.3)

where we recall that
T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}
,
R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
and
R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
are given by (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), respectively. Now, thanks to Proposition
10.1
, Lemma
6.5
and Lemma
6.1
there holds

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{Z_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})+T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{Z_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})+T_{k,y_{0},\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\omega_{k}^{0}\|_{X_{k}}+\|R_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\varrho_{k}^{0}\|_{X_{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega_{k}^{0}\|_{H^{1}_{k}}+k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\varrho_{k}^{0}\|_{L^{2}},

uniformly for all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
, and all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.
∎

With the uniform
Z_{k}
bounds at hand for
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, we are able to use (
12.1
) to upgrade them to uniform
X_{k}
bounds.

Proposition 12.2
.

Let
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
{\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})
a solution to (
12.1
). Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}}\|{\varphi}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}.

uniformly in
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\varphi_{1,1,{\mathsf{r}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\varphi_{1,1,{\mathsf{s}},k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\eta^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}+\widetilde{\varphi}_{0,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

(12.4)

where
