\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{ikt}\frac{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\partial_{z}\left(\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)\Big|_{y_{0}\in\partial B_{\delta}^{c}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{ikt}\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(z)\cap B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\frac{\partial_{z}\left(\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{ikt}\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}^{c}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\partial_{z}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\frac{\partial_{z}\left(\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ikt}\left(-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,3}\right)

We see from the local and global pointwise estimates of Proposition
12.2
that

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}.

Likewise, the local decomposition and bounds of Propositions
12.2
and
12.14
yield

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(z)\cap B_{\delta_{0}}(z)}(k|z-y_{0}|)^{-\frac{3}{2}-\mu(z)}\mathrm{d}y_{0}\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(1+(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Finally, the global pointwise bounds of Propositions
12.2
and
12.14
give

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Hence we conclude that
\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,2}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(kt)^{-1}\left(1+(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}
. Together with
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4,1}
we reach

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left((k\delta)+(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}+k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(kt)^{-1}\left(1+(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(t^{-1}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(z)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

and we obtain the same estimate for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4}
as those for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,3}
setting
\delta=\frac{\delta_{0}}{4t}
.

\bullet

End of proof.
Gathering the bounds for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,j}
, for
j=1,2,3,4
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{5}{2}}\left(t^{-2}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)t^{-1}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

thus finishing the proof.
∎

The ideas presented in the proof of the above Lemma also yield the next result.

Lemma 13.7
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(t^{-2}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)t^{-1}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

uniformly for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

We remark here that the loss of one full power of
k
decay in the estimate is due to the bounds of
\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, which are indeed one power of
k
worse than those for
\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
as can be seen from Propositions
12.2
and
12.6
. This loss can be avoided if one is willing to obtain less vanishing in
(\vartheta_{2}-y)_{+}
. More precisely, using Minkowski inequality in the global estimates for
\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}
we are able to show that

Corollary 13.8
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-2}\left(t^{-2}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-1}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

uniformly for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

13.2.
Decay estimates for
\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)

In this section we comment on the proof for the inviscid damping for
\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)
.

Proposition 13.9
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in[0,\vartheta_{1}]\cup[\vartheta_{2},2]
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proposition 13.10
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\varpi_{1,2})\cup(\varpi_{2,2}\vartheta_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proposition 13.11
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in[\varpi_{1,1},\varpi_{1,2}]\cup[\varpi_{2,1},\varpi_{2,2}]
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}\left(1+\log(t)\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proposition 13.12
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{1,2},\varpi_{2,1})
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
t\geq 1
.

To prove the above decay rates, we see from Lemma
11.6
that

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

and we proceed as usual, considering the integral over
B_{\delta}
and over
B_{\delta}^{c}
. On
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}
we can use the local estimates of Propositions
12.2
and
12.4
to gain smallness from
(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}
for
y\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\left(1-\log(k\delta)\right)
for
y\in I_{M}
. On
B_{\delta}^{c}
, we integrate by parts using the oscillatory factor and we bound the resulting contributions with the local and global estimates of Propositions
12.18
and
12.19
, roughly collecting
(kt)^{-1}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}
and
(kt)^{-1}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\left(1+\log(k\delta)\right)
for
y\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
y\in I_{M}
, respectively. Optimizing for
\delta\sim\frac{1}{kt}
gives the desired result. While we omit the routine details to the basic decay rates, we do show how to obtain the improved localised bounds for
\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)
.

Corollary 13.13
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min\left(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},t^{-1}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

for all
y\in(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle=-ikv^{\prime}(y)t\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+\frac{v^{\prime}(y)}{2\pi i}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0}))\mathrm{d}y_{0}

Using Propositions
12.6
and
12.13
and the ideas presented in the proof of Lemma
13.6
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}\right|\lesssim(kt)^{-1}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

while integration by parts once and using Propositions
12.2
and
12.18
as in the proof of the estimates of
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4}
in Lemma
13.6
yield

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0}))\mathrm{d}y_{0}\right|\lesssim(kt)^{-1}k^{-\frac{3}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{y}\psi_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(kt)k^{-\frac{3}{2}}\min\left(t^{-\frac{3}{2}+\mu(y)},t^{-2}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)t^{-1}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}+(kt)^{-1}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min\left(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},t^{-1}+(\vartheta_{2}-y)\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

and the proof is concluded.
∎

13.3.
Time-decay estimates for
\rho_{k}(t,y)

Lastly, we address the inviscid damping experienced by the density perturbation. We recall that

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{0}^{2}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\rho_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\rho_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0},

(13.6)

where we have

\displaystyle\rho_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})=\mathrm{P}(y)\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}.

From here, we readily see that
\rho_{k}(t,y)
is compactly supported in
(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})
, namely

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(t,y)=0,\quad\text{ for all }y\in[0,2]\setminus(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}).

Moreover, for all
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})
, we further observe that Lemma
11.6
and the dominated convergence theorem give

\displaystyle\rho_{k}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

To ease notation, we set

\displaystyle\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)=\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0},

(13.7)

so that now
\rho_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
. The inviscid damping in the weakly stratified regime reads

Proposition 13.14
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\varpi_{1,1})\cup(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
.

Proof.

As usual, let
\delta_{0}=\frac{3\beta}{k}
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\delta_{0}}{2})
. We have

\displaystyle\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta}(y)}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)}{ikt}{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)\Big|_{y_{0}\in\partial B_{\delta}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)}{ikt}\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(y)\cap B_{\delta_{0}}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)}{ikt}\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}^{c}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}{e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}+\frac{1}{ikt}\left(-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,3}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4}\right).

Appealing to the local decomposition and estimates of Proposition
12.2
we get

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}

as we recall that
\frac{\mathrm{P}(y)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\lesssim 1
. Likewise, Proposition
12.2
also gives

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}

because the contributions of
y_{0}=\vartheta_{1}
and
y_{0}=\vartheta_{2}
vanish in the limit thanks to Lemma
11.6
. Next, the local decomposition and estimates of Proposition
12.7
yield

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,3}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

while the global pointwise bounds of the same Proposition
12.7
provide

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,4}\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

gathering all the above estimates and optimizing for
\delta=\frac{\beta}{kt}
we get the desired result.
∎

As before, we can in fact localize the behaviour of
\rho_{k}(t,y)
, now at the cost of a logarithmic loss.

Corollary 13.15
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min\left(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},\mathrm{P}(y)(1+\log(t))\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\varpi_{1,1})\cup(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Proposition
13.14
, but now we treat

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=:{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{-}-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{+}

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(y)\cap B_{\delta_{0}}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}^{c}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)\left({\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,2}\right)

differently. Since
\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}=-\partial_{y_{0}}\log(k(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon))
, we now integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{P}(y)e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}\log(k({v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}))\Big|_{y_{0}\in B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad-ikt\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}\log(k({v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}))v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\partial_{y_{0}}{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}\log(k({v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}))\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)\left(-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}^{\pm}-ikt{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}^{\pm}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}^{\pm}\right).

The local decomposition of Proposition
12.2
gives

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}^{\pm}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\log(k\delta){\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}.

Likewise, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}^{\pm}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu(y)}\left(1+\log(k\delta)\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}

while we lastly use Proposition
12.7
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}^{\pm}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\left(1+\log(k\delta)\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}.

We remark here that
\left(\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{{\mathsf{s}}}\lesssim(1-2\mu(y))
. We next address
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}
, where we use the local decomposition of Proposition
12.2
to write

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}\int_{y+\delta}^{y+\delta_{0}}(k|y-y_{0}|)^{-1}\mathrm{d}y_{0}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{\delta}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}.

Finally, the global estimates of Proposition
12.2
provide

\displaystyle\left|\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,2}\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,0}.

Combining together the estimates and setting
\delta=\frac{\delta_{0}}{4t}
we obtain the desired result.
∎

Using the ideas of Propositions
13.1
,
13.2
and proceeding as in Proposition
13.14
we also have

Proposition 13.16
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}(1+\log(t)){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
y\in[\varpi_{1,1},\varpi_{1,2}]\cup[\varpi_{2,1},\varpi_{2,2}]
.

Proposition 13.17
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
t\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
y\in(\varpi_{1,2},\varpi_{2,1})
.

13.4.
Proof of Theorem
B

The proof of Theorem
B
follows from Parseval identity,

\displaystyle\|v^{x}(t,x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}|v^{x}_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(2\sum_{k\geq 1}|\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|v^{y}(t,x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}|v^{y}_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(2\sum_{k\geq 1}k^{2}|\psi_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\rho(t,x,y)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}|\rho_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\left(2\sum_{k\geq 1}|\rho_{k}(t,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

the decay estimates from Propositions
13.1
-
13.14

and the observations that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k>0}k^{-3}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{0}\|_{H^{1/2}_{x}H^{3}_{y}}+\|\varrho^{0}\|_{H^{1/2}_{x}H^{4}_{y}},

together with

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k>0}k^{-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{0}\|_{H^{3/2}_{x}H^{3}_{y}}+\|\varrho^{0}\|_{H^{3/2}_{x}H^{4}_{y}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k>0}k^{-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{0}\|_{H^{1/2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}}+\|\varrho^{0}\|_{H^{1/2}_{x}H^{3}_{y}}.

14.
Growth of vorticity and density gradients

In this last section we address Theorem
D
.

14.1.
Growth of vorticity

Since
\omega_{k}(t,y)=\Delta_{k}\psi_{k}(t,y)
, we have from (
13.1
) that

\displaystyle\omega_{k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

(14.1)

Proposition 14.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\varpi_{1,1})\cup(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\omega_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proof.

We observe that

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\partial_{y}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}-k^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}+2\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}-k^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}.

Moreover, it is an exercise to see that, thanks to the local and global pointwise bounds of Propositions
12.2
,
12.6
and
12.13
, there holds

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(2\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})-\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}-k^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

Hence, in view of (
14.1
), we shall consider

\displaystyle\omega_{1,k}(t,y)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

Assume
y\in(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
, let
\delta_{0}=\min\left(\frac{3\beta}{k},\frac{\varpi_{2,2}-\varpi_{2}}{2}\right)
and
\delta\in(0,\frac{\delta_{0}}{2})
. We shall assume that
\delta_{0}=\frac{3\beta}{k}
since otherwise the proof follows with obvious modifications. Given the singular behaviour of
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
described in Proposition
12.14
near the critical layer, set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}=\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}=\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

To estimate these two contributions, we shall use the ideas presented in the proof of Proposition
13.3
.

\bullet

Estimates for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}
.
For
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}
we now integrate by parts the singular
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
term, yielding

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}

\displaystyle=e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\Big|_{y_{0}\in\partial B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+ikt\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{2}\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{B_{\delta}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}+ikt{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}.

It follows from the local estimates of Proposition
12.7
that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}\right|+\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}.

Proposition
12.7
also shows that for
|y_{0}-y|=\delta
with
y_{0}>\vartheta_{2}
there holds

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

while if
y_{0}\rightarrow\vartheta_{2}\in B_{\delta}(y)
then

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left((k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}+(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu(y))(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

because

\displaystyle(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}=e^{(\mu(y)-\mu(\vartheta_{2}))\log(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)}\lesssim e^{ck^{-1}}\lesssim 1

for some
c>0
bounded and

\displaystyle\left|(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu(y))(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\right|=\left|(\mu(\vartheta_{2})-\mu(y))(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\right|\lesssim k^{-1}(k|y-\vartheta_{2}|)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\lesssim 1.

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}\left(t+(k\delta)^{-1}\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}.

\bullet

Estimates for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}
.
We further define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\delta}^{c}(y)\cap B_{\delta_{0}}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,2}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}^{c}(y)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

so that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}={\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,2}
. The global pointwise bounds of Proposition
12.14
readily give

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,2}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Similarly, we use the local estimates on
\partial_{y_{0}}^{2}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
from Proposition
12.14
to integrate the singularities and find that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{3}{2}}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Therefore, we obtain
\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{-\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

\bullet

End of proof.
Gathering the estimate of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{1,k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(k\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu(y)}(t+(k\delta)^{-1}){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

Optimizing in
\delta>0
, we find that
\delta=\frac{\beta}{kt}
gives the desired result.
∎

As before, we can further localise the estimates near the fragile regime, losing some time-decay.

Proposition 14.2
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{2,2},2]
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{k}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}\min(1+(\vartheta_{2}-y)_{+}t,t^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proof.

Similar to the arguments that lead to Proposition
13.5
, we now compute

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\left(e^{ikv(y)t}\omega_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(ikv^{\prime}(y)t\omega_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+\partial_{y}\omega_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right)e^{ikv(y)t}

\displaystyle=ikv^{\prime}(y)te^{ikv(y)t}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+e^{ikv(y)t}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-e^{ikv(y)t}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

Integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle-\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=-e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\Big|_{y_{0}=\vartheta_{1}}^{y_{0}=\vartheta_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-ikt\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{2}\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}.

Moreover, due to the support assumptions on the initial data,
v^{\prime\prime}(y)
and
{\mathcal{P}}(y)
we further have from Lemma
11.6
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(y))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\Big|_{y_{0}=\vartheta_{1}}^{y_{0}=\vartheta_{2}}\mathrm{d}y=0.

Hence, since
\omega_{k}(t,\vartheta_{2})=0
, see Proposition
14.5
below, we can write

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\omega_{k}(t,y)=-\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\left(ikt\omega_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+\omega_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+\omega_{3,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right)

where

\displaystyle\omega_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(z)-v^{\prime}(y_{0}))v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\omega_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\omega_{3,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle:=\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}\mathrm{d}z.

Appealing to Proposition
14.1
, it is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{3,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu(z)}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\vartheta_{2}-y)t{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

while we can argue similarly, with the help of (
12.1
), Proposition
12.2
and
{\mathcal{P}}(y)\lesssim(1-2\mu(y))
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\omega_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\vartheta_{2}-y){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Now, for
\omega_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
, let
\delta_{0}=\frac{3\beta}{k}
, we write

\displaystyle\omega_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)=\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}\left({\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}(t,z)+{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}(t,z)\right)\mathrm{d}z

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}(t,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}(t,z)

\displaystyle:=\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}^{c}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

We can easily use the global bounds of Proposition
12.6
to have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,2}(t,z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}(\vartheta_{2}-y){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

Instead, for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}
we note that

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})=\partial_{y}\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})-k^{2}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})

so that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\partial_{y_{0}}\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y_{0}}\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-k^{2}\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}

Using the local estimates of Propositions
12.6
and
12.13
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,1}\right|+\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,3}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

For
{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}
we note that
|\partial_{y_{0}}\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}|
is too singular to be integrated directly. Hence, we integrate the
\partial_{y_{0}}
derivative,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}

\displaystyle=e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\Big|_{y_{0}\in\partial B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+ikt\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{2}\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{B_{\delta_{0}}(z)\cap(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})}e^{-ik(v(y_{0})-v(z))t}\left(\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(z,y_{0})-\partial_{y}\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(z,y_{0})\right)v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2,1}+ikt{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2,2}-{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2,3}

According to the local estimates of Proposition
12.6
we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2,1}\right|\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1},\quad\sum_{j=2}^{3}\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2,j}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

from which we conclude that
\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1,2}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}
, for all
t\geq 1
. Therefore,
\left|{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}
and thus

\displaystyle\left|\int_{y}^{\vartheta_{2}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\varepsilon,1}(t,z)\mathrm{d}z\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\vartheta_{2}-y)t{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}.

With this, the proposition follows.
∎

Following the ideas of Proposition
14.1
, it is an exercise to prove

Proposition 14.3
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in[\varpi_{1,1},\varpi_{1,2}]\cup[\varpi_{2,1},\varpi_{2,2}]
. Then,

\displaystyle|\omega_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}(1+\log(t)){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

for all
t\geq 1
.

We also have

Proposition 14.4
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{1,2},\varpi_{2,1})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\omega_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2},

for all
t\geq 1
.

Moreover, due to the compact support of the initial data and
v^{\prime\prime}(y)
and
{\mathcal{P}}(y)
, we have from (
14.1
) and (
12.1
) the following.

Proposition 14.5
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Then,
\omega_{k}(t,y)=0
, for all
y\in[0,\vartheta_{1}]\cup[\vartheta_{2},2]
and
t\geq 1
.

14.2.
Growth of density gradients

We now show how to obtain the growth bounds on the density gradient. We shall prove them for
\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)
. We now have

Proposition 14.6
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\vartheta_{0},\varpi_{1,1})\cup(\varpi_{2,2},\vartheta_{2})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathrm{P}^{\prime}(y)(1+\log(t))+t\min(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},\mathrm{P}(y)(1+\log(t))\right){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

for all
t\geq 1
.

Proof.

In view of (
13.6
) and (
13.7
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{P^{\prime}}(y)}{\mathrm{P}(y)}\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\partial_{y_{0}}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{P^{\prime}}(y)}{\mathrm{P}(y)}\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+\rho_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)-\rho_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y).

It is immediate from Corollary
13.15
that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathrm{P^{\prime}}(y)}{\mathrm{P}(y)}\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{P^{\prime}}(y)}{\mathrm{P}(y)}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},\mathrm{P}(y)(1+\log(t))){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{P}^{\prime}(y)k^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\log(t)){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}.

We treat
\rho_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
and
\rho_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
separately.

\bullet

Estimates for
\rho_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
.
We crucially observe that

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{y}+\partial_{y_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon)^{2}}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{(v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)(v^{\prime}(y)-v^{\prime}(y_{0})).

Hence, we can argue as in the proof of Corollary
13.15
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},\mathrm{P}(y)(1+\log(t))){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

for all
t\geq 1
. We remark here that in the integration by parts argument of Corollary
13.15
performed to keep
\mathrm{P}(y)
, we shall now obtain a term of the form

\displaystyle v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\log(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)=\frac{v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})}{2v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\partial_{y_{0}}\log^{2}(v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)

which can be further integrated by parts to obtain the desired regularity, which in turn is translated to the appropriate time decay, we omit the details.

\bullet

Estimates for
\rho_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
.
We now integrate by parts the
\partial_{y_{0}}
derivative to obtain

\displaystyle\rho_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{P}(y)e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\Big|_{y_{0}=\vartheta_{1}}^{y_{0}=\vartheta_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+ikt\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{0})\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{v^{\prime}(y_{0})\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{\vartheta_{1}}^{\vartheta_{2}}e^{-ikv(y_{0})t}\left(\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{-}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})-i\varepsilon}-\frac{\varphi_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y,y_{0})}{v(y)-v(y_{0})+i\varepsilon}\right)v^{\prime\prime}(y_{0})\mathrm{d}y_{0}

\displaystyle=\rho_{3,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)+ikt\rho_{4,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)-\rho_{5,k,\varepsilon}(t,y).

For
y\in(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})
it is immediate from Lemma
11.6
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\rho_{3,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)=0.

Moreover, we can directly use the ideas of Corollary
13.15
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{4,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|+\left|\rho_{5,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)\right|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\min(t^{-\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)},\mathrm{P}(y)(1+\log(t))){\mathcal{S}}_{k,1}

for all
t\geq 1
.

\bullet

End of proof.
Combining the estimates for
\rho_{k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
, with those for
\rho_{1,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
and
\rho_{2,k,\varepsilon}(t,y)
we obtain the claimed result.
∎

For the other regimes we do not need to consider the localised bounds, so that the ideas of Proposition
14.6
provide the next two results.

Proposition 14.7
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in[\varpi_{1,1},\varpi_{1,2}]\cup[\varpi_{2,1},\varpi_{2,2}]
. Then,

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu(y)}(1+\log(t)){\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

for all
t\geq 1
.

For the strong regime, we have

Proposition 14.8
.

Let
k\geq 1
and
y\in(\varpi_{1,2},\varpi_{2,1})
. Then,

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)|\lesssim k^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}

for all
t\geq 1
.

14.3.
Proof of Theorem
D

Just as for Theorem
B
, the growth estimates from Theorem
D
and Theorem
E
are a consequence of Propositions
14.1
-
14.8
, Parseval identity, and the observations that

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k>0}k^{-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{0}\|_{H^{3/2}_{x}H^{3}_{y}}+\|\widetilde{q}^{0}\|_{H^{3/2}_{x}H^{4}_{y}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k>0}k{\mathcal{S}}_{k,2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{0}\|_{H^{5/2}_{x}H^{3}_{y}}+\|\widetilde{q}^{0}\|_{H^{5/2}_{x}H^{4}_{y}},

we omit the details.

Remark 14.9
.

As we commented in Section
2
, we can use (
2.1
) and Propositions
13.1
-
13.4
and Propositions
13.14
-
13.17
to prove the growth bounds of Theorem
D
. To do that, we note that

\displaystyle e^{ikv(y)t}\rho_{k}(t,y)=\rho_{k}^{0}(y)+ik\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{0}^{t}\psi_{k}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s

for all
t\geq 0
. Hence,

\displaystyle e^{ikv(y)t}\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)

\displaystyle=-ikv(y)t\rho_{k}(t,y)+\partial_{y}\rho_{k}^{0}(y)

\displaystyle\quad+ik\mathrm{P}(y)\int_{0}^{t}e^{ikv(y)s}\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s+ik\mathrm{P}^{\prime}(y)\int_{0}^{t}e^{ikv(y)s}\psi_{k}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s

from which the growth rate for
\partial_{y}\rho_{k}(t,y)
follows from the decay estimates on
\rho_{k}(t,y)
,
\psi_{k}(t,y)
and
\partial_{y}\psi_{k}(t,y)
. On the other hand, from the vorticity equation we note that

\displaystyle e^{ikv(y)t}\omega_{k}(t,y)=\omega_{k}^{0}(y)+ikv^{\prime\prime}(y)\int_{0}^{t}e^{ikv(y)s}\psi_{k}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s-ik{\mathfrak{g}}\int_{0}^{t}e^{ikv(y)s}\rho_{k}(s,y)\mathrm{d}s

and thus the growth bounds for
\omega_{k}(t,y)
are obtained once we integrate the decay estimates for
\psi_{k}(t,y)
and
\rho_{k}(t,y)
.

Appendix A
Logarithmic approximations

In this section we state and prove two simple identities involving limiting regimes that give rise to logarithmic corrections.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
{\rm Re}(\gamma)\geq 0
and
|\gamma|\leq 1
. Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{2\gamma}-1}{2\gamma}=\log(\zeta){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma}(\zeta),\quad{\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma}(\zeta):=\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma s\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s

and we further have that
\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\|{\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R}(0))}\lesssim_{R}1
.

Proof.

The fundamental theorem of calculus shows that

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{2\gamma}-1}{2\gamma}=\frac{1}{2\gamma}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}e^{2\gamma s\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s=\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma s\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s.

Since
\log(\zeta)=\log(|\zeta|)+i\text{Arg}(\zeta)
, the bound follows from the fact that
{\rm Re}(2\gamma s\log(\zeta))\lesssim 1+\log R
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
{\rm Re}(\gamma)\geq 0
and
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{16}
. Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{2\gamma}-\zeta^{-2\gamma}}{2\gamma}=2\log(\zeta)+\mathrm{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)

and we further have
\sup_{|\gamma|\leq 1}\|\gamma^{-2}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R}(0))}\lesssim 1
.

Proof.

We note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta^{2\gamma}-\zeta^{-2\gamma}}{2\gamma}-2\log(\zeta)

\displaystyle=2\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma(2u-1)\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}u-2\log(\zeta)

\displaystyle=2\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}\left(e^{2\gamma(2u-1)\log(\zeta)}-1\right)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=2\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}2\gamma(2u-1)\log(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma(2u-1)r\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=8\gamma^{2}\log^{3}(\zeta)\int_{0}^{1}(2u-1)^{2}\int_{0}^{1}r\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma(2u-1)rs\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}u

because
\int_{0}^{1}(2u-1)\mathrm{d}u=0
. Hence, we see that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4\gamma^{2}}\mathrm{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)\right|\lesssim|\zeta|^{\frac{1}{4}}|\log(\zeta)|^{3}\lesssim 1

since
{\rm Re}(\tfrac{1}{4}-2\gamma rs)>\tfrac{1}{8}
, for all
r,s\in(0,1)
and all
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{16}
. In particular,
\left|\frac{\mathrm{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)}{4\gamma^{2}}\right|
is uniformly bounded whenever
|\zeta|
is uniformly bounded from below.
∎

Appendix B
Properties of the Whittaker functions

Here we state and prove some properties of the Whittaker functions that are used throughout the paper, we refer to
[
NIST
]
for a complete description of the Whittaker functions. For
\gamma,\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
, the Whittaker function
M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)
is given by

M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\zeta}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma}M\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta\right),\quad M(a,b,\zeta)=\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\frac{(a)_{s}}{(b)_{s}s!}\zeta^{s},

where
(a)_{s}=a(a+1)(a+2)\dots(a+s-1)
denotes the Pochhammer symbol. For
\gamma\neq 0
, we also introduce the Whittaker function

W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)=\sqrt{\frac{\zeta}{\pi}}K_{\gamma}(\zeta/2)

where
K_{\gamma}
denotes the second modified Bessel function. It is such that

\displaystyle K_{\gamma}(\zeta)=\frac{\pi}{2}\frac{I_{-\gamma}(\zeta)-I_{\gamma}(\zeta)}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}

where
I_{\gamma}
stands for the first modified Bessel function and it is given by the series representation

\displaystyle I_{\gamma}(\zeta)=\left(\frac{\zeta}{2}\right)^{\gamma}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\zeta/2)^{n}}{\Gamma(\gamma+n+1)}.

For
\gamma=0
, we instead define

W_{0,0}(\zeta):=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\zeta}\sqrt{\frac{\zeta}{\pi}}\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\right)_{s}}{(s!)^{2}}\zeta^{s}\left(2\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(1+s)}{\Gamma(1+s)}-\frac{\Gamma^{\prime}(\tfrac{1}{2}+s)}{\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+s)}-\log(\zeta)\right),

where
\Gamma(x)
denotes the Gamma function. The Whittaker functions
M_{0,\gamma}
and
W_{0,\gamma}
are solutions to the Taylor-Goldstein equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\zeta}^{2}\phi(\zeta)+\left(-\frac{1}{4}+\frac{\frac{1}{4}-\gamma^{2}}{\zeta^{2}}\right)\phi(\zeta)=0.

For
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
we next set
\gamma=\mu+i\nu
. We begin by recording some basic properties regarding complex conjugation for
M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)
, which can be deduce from the series definition of
M_{0,\gamma}
.

Lemma B.1
.

We have the following

•

For
\beta^{2}>1/4
, then
M_{0,i\nu}(\zeta)=\overline{M_{0,-i\nu}\left(\overline{\zeta}\right)}
.

•

For
\beta^{2}\leq 1/4
, then
M_{0,\mu}(\zeta)=\overline{M_{0,\mu}\left(\overline{\zeta}\right)}
. Additionally, for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
then
M_{0,\mu}(x),W_{0,0}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

We next state an analytic continuation property, which is key in studying the Wronskian of the Green’s function and is directly determined by the analytic continuation of the non-entire terms of
M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)
and
W_{0,\gamma}
.

Lemma B.2

(
[
NIST
]
)
.

Let
\beta^{2}>0
. Then

M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta{\rm e}^{\pm\pi i})=\pm i{\rm e}^{\pm\gamma\pi i}M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta),\quad W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta e^{\pm i\pi})=\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\gamma)}{\Gamma(1+2\gamma)}M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)\pm ie^{\mp\gamma\pi i}W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)

for all
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
.

The next result gives a precise description of the asymptotic expansion of
M_{\pm}(\zeta)
and its derivatives, for
\zeta
in a bounded domain.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
\gamma=\mu+i\nu
with
0\leq\mu<\frac{1}{2}
and
0\leq\nu
. Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
B_{R}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
denote the closed unit ball of radius
R>0
centered in the origin. Then,

\displaystyle M_{0,\pm\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\gamma}\mathcal{E}_{0,\pm\gamma}(\zeta),

where
\mathcal{E}_{0,\pm\gamma}\in C^{\infty}(B_{R})
, with
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}(0)=1
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\|\mathcal{E}_{0,\pm\gamma}\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim_{\gamma,R}1
.

Proof.

From
[
NIST
]
we know that

\displaystyle M_{0,\pm\gamma}(\zeta)=2^{\frac{1}{2}\pm 2\gamma}\Gamma(1\pm\gamma)\sqrt{\tfrac{\zeta}{2}}I_{\pm\gamma}(\tfrac{\zeta}{2})

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\gamma}\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{\Gamma(1\pm\gamma)}{j!\Gamma(j+1\pm\gamma)}\left(\tfrac{\zeta}{4}\right)^{2j}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}\pm\gamma}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}(\zeta)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}
is an entire function in
{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}(0)=1
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\pm\gamma}^{\prime}(0)=0
. In particular,
\|\mathcal{E}_{0,\pm\gamma}\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim_{R}C_{\gamma}
, with
C_{\gamma}>0
uniformly bounded for
|\gamma|
bounded and
|\mu|\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
. Let
B_{R}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
denote the closed unit ball of radius
R>0
centered in the origin. Then,

\displaystyle W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma}{\mathcal{E}}_{1,\gamma}(\zeta)-\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\log(\zeta)\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta){\mathcal{E}}_{2,\gamma}(\zeta),

where we recall

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma}(\zeta)=\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma s\log(\zeta)}\mathrm{d}s

and
{\mathcal{E}}_{j,\gamma}(\zeta)
are entire functions in
{\mathbb{C}}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{j,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\|{\mathcal{E}}_{j,\gamma}\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim 1
, for
j=1,2
uniformly for
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
.

Proof.

We begin by noting that

\displaystyle W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\zeta}{\pi}}K_{\gamma}(\tfrac{\zeta}{2})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\pi\zeta}\frac{I_{\gamma}(\tfrac{\zeta}{2})-I_{-\gamma}(\tfrac{\zeta}{2})}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}\left(\frac{\zeta^{\gamma}}{4^{\gamma}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}+\gamma\right)}\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{\frac{\zeta}{2}\cos\theta}(\sin\theta)^{2\gamma}\mathrm{d}\theta-\frac{\zeta^{-\gamma}}{4^{-\gamma}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-\gamma\right)}\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{\frac{\zeta}{2}\cos\theta}(\sin\theta)^{-2\gamma}\mathrm{d}\theta\right).

For

\displaystyle f_{1}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4^{\gamma}\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\gamma)},\quad f_{2}(\gamma,\zeta)=\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{\frac{\zeta}{2}\cos\theta}(\sin\theta)^{2\gamma}\mathrm{d}\theta,\quad f_{3}(\gamma)=\zeta^{\gamma},

together with

\displaystyle f_{1}(\gamma)f_{2}(\gamma)f_{3}(\gamma)-f_{1}(-\gamma)f_{2}(-\gamma)f_{3}(-\gamma)

\displaystyle=\left(f_{1}(\gamma)-f_{1}(-\gamma)\right)f_{2}(\gamma)f_{3}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\quad+f_{1}(-\gamma)\left(f_{2}(\gamma)-f_{2}(-\gamma)\right)f_{3}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\quad+f_{1}(-\gamma)f_{2}(-\gamma)\left(f_{3}(\gamma)-f_{3}(-\gamma)\right).

we now have

\displaystyle W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma}{\mathcal{E}}_{1,\gamma}(\zeta)+\zeta^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\log(\zeta){\mathcal{Q}}_{\gamma}(\zeta){\mathcal{E}}_{2,\gamma}(\zeta)

where we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1,\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}\left(f_{2}(\gamma,\zeta)\frac{f_{1}(\gamma)-f_{1}(-\gamma)}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}+f_{1}(-\gamma)\frac{f_{2}(\gamma,\zeta)-f_{2}(-\gamma,\zeta)}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{2,\gamma}(\zeta)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\gamma}{\sin(\gamma\pi)}f_{1}(-\gamma)f_{2}(-\gamma,\zeta).

Firstly, we note that
\partial_{\zeta}f_{2}(\gamma,0)=0
for all
0<|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
. Hence,
{\mathcal{E}}_{j,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)=0
, for all
0<|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
. Secondly, both
f_{1}(\gamma)
and
\partial_{\zeta}^{n}f_{2}(\gamma,\zeta)
are uniformly bounded for
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
and
|\zeta|\leq R
, for
n=0,1,2
. With this, we see that
\|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,\gamma}\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
.

On the other hand, there holds
f_{1}(\gamma)-f_{1}(-\gamma)=\gamma f_{1,\infty}(\gamma)
, with
\|f_{1,\infty}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\frac{1}{4}}(0))}\lesssim 1
. Similarly,

\displaystyle f_{2}(\gamma,\zeta)-f_{2}(-\gamma,\zeta)=4\gamma\int_{0}^{\pi}e^{\frac{\zeta}{2}\cos\theta}(\sin\theta)^{-2\gamma}\log(\sin\theta)Q_{2\gamma}(\sin\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta

Since
|\sin(\theta)|\leq 1
and
x^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log x
is integrable, we see that
|{\mathcal{Q}}_{2\gamma}(\sin\theta)|\lesssim 1
and

\displaystyle\|f_{2}(\gamma,\cdot)-f_{2}(-\gamma,\cdot)\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim\gamma.

for all
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
. With this, we conclude that
\|{\mathcal{E}}_{1,\gamma}\|_{C^{2}(B_{R})}\lesssim 1
uniformly in
|\gamma|\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
and the proof is complete.
∎

In the future, for
f
smooth and compactly supported, we shall need

\displaystyle\int\frac{f(z)}{z}\log(z)\mathcal{Q}_{\gamma}(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int\log(z)f(z)\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma u\log(z)}-1}{2\gamma u}\right)\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\int f(z)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{e^{2\gamma u\log(z)}-1}{2\gamma u}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\int f^{\prime}(z)\log^{2}(z)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma ur\log(z)}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int f^{\prime}(z)\log^{2}(z)\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma ur\log(z)}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\int f^{\prime}(z)\log^{2}(z)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}e^{2\gamma ur\log(z)}\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}z

which is uniformly bounded in
\gamma
for
|\gamma|\leq\frac{1}{4}
, say, by
\|f\|_{W^{1,\infty}}
.

Appendix C
The Green’s function for the mild stratified regime

Here we prove the main estimates for the Green’s function
{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)
for
y_{0}\in I_{M}
.
Firstly, we note that for
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\gamma}:=\frac{\Gamma(1+2\gamma)}{\Gamma(1/2+\gamma)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}+O(|\gamma|)

as
\gamma\rightarrow 0
. We recall that
\gamma_{0}=\gamma(y_{0})
and

{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0}):=-2k\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}\Big(M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\Big).

Using the analytic continuation properties of
M_{0,\gamma}
and
W_{0,\gamma}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})

\displaystyle=2kM_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})

\displaystyle\quad-2ki\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}\Big(e^{{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})-e^{-{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})\Big)

Lemma C.1
.

Let
y_{0}\in I_{S}
. There exists
C>0
,
\varepsilon_{*}>0
and
\gamma_{*}>0
such that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})|\geq Ck|M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})||M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})|

for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
and all
0<|{\gamma_{0}}|<\gamma_{*}
.

Proof.

It is useful to write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})

\displaystyle=2kM_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(1+i\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}\left(e^{-{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}-e^{{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)\right).

We treat two cases according to the size of the wave-number
k
. Let
N_{0}>0
be given by Lemma
C.3
.

\bullet

Case 1.
Assume
k<\vartheta^{-1}\frac{N}{2}
. Since
y_{0}
and
2-y_{0}
are bounded away from zero for all
y_{0}\in I_{M}
, we observe that for
{\gamma_{0}}=i\nu_{0}\in i{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
there holds

\displaystyle\left|1+i\Gamma_{\nu_{0}}\left(e^{{\nu_{0}}\pi}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}-e^{-{\nu_{0}}\pi}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 1-|\Gamma_{\nu_{0}}|e^{{\nu_{0}}\pi}\left|{\rm Im}\left(\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)\right|-|\Gamma_{\nu_{0}}|e^{-{\nu_{0}}\pi}\left|{\rm Im}\left(\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)\right|

The desired bound follows from Lemma
C.4
. Likewise, for
\gamma_{0}=\mu_{0}>0
we now observe that

\displaystyle\left|1+i\Gamma_{{\mathsf{r}}}\left(e^{-{\mu_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0})}-e^{{\mu_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 1-\Gamma_{0}\sin({\mu_{0}}\pi)\left(\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0})}+\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0})}\right)+O({\mu_{0}}).

We recall that
M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(\cdot)=M_{0,\mu_{0}}(2k\cdot)
is real-valued for real arguments, continuous, uniformly bounded above and also uniformly bounded away from 0 for
{\mu_{0}}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
, and that
W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(\cdot)=W_{0,\mu_{0}}(\cdot)
is also real-valued for real arguments, continuous and bounded uniformly for
{\mu_{0}}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right)
and for its argument in compact sets away from 0, see
C.2
and Lemma
C.4
. Hence, for
\varepsilon>0
small enough and
{\mu_{0}}>0
small enough, the desired bound follows.

\bullet

Case 2.
Assume
k\geq\vartheta^{-1}\frac{N}{2}
. Since both
y_{0}\geq\vartheta
and
2-y_{0}\geq\vartheta
, we now have
2ky_{0}\geq N
and also
2k(2-y_{0})\geq N
. Hence, for
\gamma_{0}=\mu_{0}+i\nu_{0}
, since

\displaystyle\left|1+i\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}\left(e^{-{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}-e^{{\gamma_{0}}\pi i}\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\geq 1-|\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}|e^{\nu_{0}\pi}\left|\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})}\right|-|\Gamma_{\gamma_{0}}|e^{-\nu_{0}\pi}\left|\frac{W_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{{\mathsf{r}}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right|,

The result follows once we use Lemma
C.3
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
and
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
. For any compact set
K\subset{\mathbb{C}}
, there holds

\displaystyle M(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)

\displaystyle=M(1/2,1,\zeta)+O(\gamma).

uniformly for all
\zeta\in K
. Moreover, if
K\subset\{{\rm Re}(\zeta)>0\}
and it is uniformly bounded away from
0
, then

\displaystyle U(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)=U(1/2,1,\zeta)+O(\gamma)

uniformly for all
\zeta\in K
.

Proof.

From
[
NIST
]
, we note that

\displaystyle M(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\Gamma(1+2\gamma)\mathbf{M}(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(1+2\gamma)}{\Gamma(1/2+\gamma)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}e^{\zeta s}\left(s(1-s)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}+i\gamma}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\pi}+O(|\gamma|)\right)\int_{0}^{1}e^{\zeta s}\left(s(1-s)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left(\frac{1}{\pi}+O(|\gamma|)\right)\int_{0}^{1}e^{\zeta s}\left(s(1-s)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\log(s(1-s))\right)\int_{0}^{1}(s(1-s))^{\gamma u}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s

so that for
\zeta
bounded, we reach

\displaystyle M(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}e^{\zeta s}\left(s(1-s)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s+O(|\gamma|)=M(1/2,1,\zeta)+O(|\gamma|).

Similarly, for
{\rm Re}(\zeta)>0
,

\displaystyle U(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,\zeta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(1/2+\gamma)}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\zeta s}(s(1-s))^{-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}+O(|\gamma|)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\zeta s}(s(1-s))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}+O(|\gamma|)\right)\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\zeta s}(s(1-s))^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log(s(1-s))\int_{0}^{1}(s(1-s))^{\gamma u}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}s.

and the lemma follows for
|\zeta|
uniformly bounded from below.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Let
\gamma\in{\mathbb{C}}
. There exists
N_{0}>0
and
\gamma_{*}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)}{M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)}\right|\lesssim e^{-{\rm Re}(\zeta)},

for all
{\rm Re}(\zeta)>N
and all
|\gamma|<\gamma_{*}
.

Proof.

Let
a=\frac{1}{2}+\gamma
. We recall from
[
NIST
]
that for
a\in{\mathbb{C}}
and
{\rm Re}(\zeta)>0
,

\displaystyle M(a,2a,\zeta)=i\frac{\Gamma(2a)}{\Gamma(a)}\left(e^{\gamma\pi i}U(a,2a,\zeta)-e^{\zeta}e^{-\gamma\pi i}U(a,2a,e^{-i\pi}\zeta)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{W_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)}{M_{0,\gamma}(\zeta)}=i\frac{\Gamma(a)}{\Gamma(2a)}\frac{1}{e^{-\gamma\pi i}e^{\zeta}\frac{U(a,2a,e^{-i\pi}\zeta)}{U(a,2a,\zeta)}-e^{\gamma\pi i}}.

Moreover, one can see from 13.7.5 in
[
NIST
]
that

\displaystyle U(a,2a,\zeta)=\zeta^{-a}\left(1+O\left(|\zeta|^{-1}\right)\right)

for all
|\zeta|>1
uniformly in
a
, for bounded
|a|
. The lemma follows taking
{\rm Re}(\zeta)>0
large enough.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\vartheta=\min(\vartheta_{1},2-\vartheta_{2})
and
y_{0}\in[\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2}]
such that
2ky_{0}\leq N_{0}
. Then, there exists
\varepsilon_{*}>0
and
\gamma_{*}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{W_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}{M_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}-\frac{W_{0,0}(y_{0})}{M_{0,}(y_{0})}\right|\leq\epsilon,

(C.1)

for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{*}
and all
0<|\gamma|\leq\gamma_{*}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\left|{\rm Im}\left(\frac{W_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}{M_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

We observe that

\displaystyle\frac{W_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}{M_{0,\gamma}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon)}=\frac{U(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,2k(y_{0}-i\varepsilon))}{M(1/2+\gamma,1+2\gamma,2k(y_{0}-i\varepsilon))}.

Since
M(1/2,1,\cdot)
is real-valued, uniformly continuous, and non-zero, and
U(1/2,1,\cdot)
is real-valued, uniformly continuous and bounded away from 0, then the Lemma follows from the asymptotic expansions in Lemma
C.2
.
∎

Appendix D
The Green’s function for the fragile stratification

Here we show the main modifications required to obtain bounds on the Wronskian of the Green’s function associated with the fragile stratification regime. We begin with the following.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\gamma=\mu\in\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}\right)
. Then,

\lim_{{\rm Re}(\zeta)\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{M_{0,-\mu}(\zeta)}{M_{0,\mu}(\zeta)}=2^{-4\mu}\frac{\Gamma(1-\mu)}{\Gamma(1+\mu)}

Proof.

Let
\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}
, for
a_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}\pm\mu
and
b_{\pm}=2a_{\pm}
we recall that

\displaystyle M_{0,\pm\mu}(\zeta)

\displaystyle={\rm e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\zeta}\zeta^{a_{\pm}}\frac{\Gamma(b_{\pm})}{\Gamma(a_{\pm})}\left({\rm e}^{-i\pi a_{\pm}}U(a_{\pm},b_{\pm},\zeta)+{\rm e}^{i\pi a_{\pm}}{\rm e}^{\zeta}U(a_{\pm},b_{\pm},{\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)\right).

Moreover, we have that
U(a_{\pm},b_{\pm},\zeta)=\zeta^{-a_{\pm}}+\mathcal{E}_{\pm}(\zeta)
, where further

|\zeta^{a_{\pm}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}(\zeta)|\leq\frac{2\beta^{2}}{|\zeta|}{\rm e}^{\frac{2\beta^{2}}{|\zeta|}}.

with
\beta^{2}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}-\mu^{2}}
. Therefore,

M_{0,\pm\mu}(\zeta)=\frac{\Gamma(b_{\pm})}{\Gamma(a_{\pm})}{\rm e}^{\frac{1}{2}\zeta}\left(\left[1+({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)^{a_{\pm}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)\right]+{\rm e}^{-\zeta}{\rm e}^{-i\pi a_{\pm}}\left[1+\zeta^{a_{\pm}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}(\zeta)\right]\right)

Now, since
b_{\pm}=2a_{\pm}
, we have from
[
NIST
]
that

\frac{\Gamma(b_{\pm})}{\Gamma(a_{\pm})}=\pi^{-\frac{1}{2}}2^{2a_{\pm}-1}\Gamma\left(a_{\pm}+\frac{1}{2}\right),

and thus

\frac{\displaystyle{\Gamma(b_{-})}/{\Gamma(a_{-})}}{\displaystyle{\Gamma(b_{+})}/{\Gamma(a_{+})}}=2^{-4\mu}\frac{\Gamma(1-\mu)}{\Gamma(1+\mu)}.

Hence,

\frac{M_{0,-\mu}(\zeta)}{M_{0,\mu}(\zeta)}=2^{-4\mu}\frac{\Gamma(1-\mu)}{\Gamma(1+\mu)}\frac{1+({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)^{a_{-}}{\mathcal{E}}_{-}({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)+{\rm e}^{-\zeta}{\rm e}^{-i\pi a_{-}}\left[1+\zeta^{a_{-}}{\mathcal{E}}_{-}(\zeta)\right]}{1+({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)^{a_{+}}{\mathcal{E}}_{+}({\rm e}^{i\pi}\zeta)+{\rm e}^{-\zeta}{\rm e}^{-i\pi a_{+}}\left[1+\zeta^{a_{+}}{\mathcal{E}}_{+}(\zeta)\right]}.

Since
|\zeta^{a_{\pm}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\pm}(\zeta)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|\zeta|}
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\gamma=\mu\in\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}\right)
. Let
\zeta=x+iy
, with
\zeta\in B_{R}
and
x\geq c_{0}>0
, for some
c_{0}>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|M_{0,-\mu}(x+iy)-M_{0,-\mu}(x)\right|\lesssim\frac{|y|}{x}

Proof.

Recall that
M_{0,-\mu}(\zeta)=\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\mathcal{E}_{2,-\mu}(\zeta)
with

\displaystyle(x+iy)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}-x^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}

\displaystyle=(x+iy)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}-1-\left(x^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}-1\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu\right)\left(\log(x+iy)\int_{0}^{1}e^{(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)u\log(x+iy)}\mathrm{d}u-\log(x)\int_{0}^{1}e^{(\frac{1}{2}-\mu)u\log(x)}\mathrm{d}u\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu\right)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{y}{x+iys}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(1+u\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu\right)\log(x+iys)\right)e^{u\left(\frac{1}{2}-\mu\right)\log(x+iys)}\mathrm{d}u\right)\mathrm{d}s.

Since
|\zeta|\leq R
and
x\geq c_{0}>0
we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|(x+iy)^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}-x^{\frac{1}{2}-\mu}\right|\lesssim\frac{|y|}{x}.

and as
\mathcal{E}_{2,-\mu}(\zeta)
is entire in
\zeta
uniformly for
\mu\in\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}\right)
, the lemma follows.
∎

We next obtain suitable lower bounds for the Wronskian of the Green’s function, which we recall takes the form

{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y_{0}):=-4k\mu_{0}\Big(M_{\mathsf{r}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0})\Big).

The analytic continuation properties of
M_{\mathsf{r}}
and
M_{\mathsf{s}}
then give

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})

\displaystyle=-4ki\mu_{0}\left({\rm e}^{i\mu_{0}\pi}M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})-{\rm e}^{-i\mu_{0}\pi}M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})\right).

We are now in position to prove the main lower bounds for
{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})
.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
\gamma_{0}=\mu_{0}\in\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}\right)
. There exists
N>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})\right|\geq 4k\mu_{0}\sin(\mu_{0}\pi)|M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})||M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})|,

for all
k\geq\vartheta^{-1}\frac{N}{2}
. Likewise, for all
k<\vartheta^{-1}\frac{N}{2}
there exists
\varepsilon_{*}>0
, independent of
\mu
such that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})\right|\geq 2k\mu_{0}\sin(\mu_{0}\pi)\left(M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0})-M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0})\right)>0,

for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
.

Proof.

To prove the first statement, recall that
y_{0}\in(\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2})
and
\vartheta=\min(\vartheta_{1},2-\vartheta_{2})
so that
2ky_{0},\,2k(2-y_{0})\geq 2k\vartheta\geq N
, for all
k\geq\vartheta^{-1}\frac{N}{2}
. Moreover, note that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})

\displaystyle=-4ki\mu_{0}M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})\left(e^{i\mu\pi}-e^{-i\mu\pi}\frac{M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}\right)

with further

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}{M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0})}=1+O(N^{-1})

due to Lemma
D.1
. The first statement follows taking
N
large enough. For the second statement, since
y_{0},\,2-y_{0}\geq\vartheta>0
and
2k|y_{0}-i\varepsilon_{0}|\leq\vartheta^{-1}(N+1)
and
2k|1-y_{0}+i\varepsilon_{0}|\leq\vartheta^{-1}(N+1)
for
\varepsilon_{0}
small enough, we have from Lemma
D.2
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})

\displaystyle=-4ki\mu_{0}\left({\rm e}^{i\mu_{0}\pi}M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0})-{\rm e}^{-i\mu_{0}\pi}M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0})+O(\varepsilon)\right)

so that, in particular,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{W}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{+}(y_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\geq 4k\mu_{0}\sin(\mu_{0}\pi)\left(M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0})+M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0})\right)+kO(\varepsilon)

The Lemma follows for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, since
M_{\mathsf{r}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{s}}(2-y_{0})+M_{\mathsf{s}}(y_{0})M_{\mathsf{r}}(2-y_{0})
is strictly positive bounded away from zero, uniformly for
\mu\in\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}\right)
and for
\vartheta_{1}\leq y_{0}\leq\vartheta_{2}
.
∎

Appendix E
High-order operator estimates for weak and strong stratifications

In this section we state and prove further several mapping properties of the maps
R_{j,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, for
j=0,1,2,3,4
that are needed to obtain Sobolev regularity on the spectral density function. To avoid repetition, we shall only prove the local
X_{k}^{1}
bounds of the operator norms, since the global
H_{k}^{1}(I_{3}(y_{0}))
estimates follow form the usual entanglement inequality. Moreover, to avoid repetition, we shall restrict ourselves to the
X^{0}_{k}
bounds, since the strategy to prove the full
X_{k}^{1}
bounds is the same.

E.1.
Second order operator estimates

We begin the section showing the mapping properties of
R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
,
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
and
f(y,y_{0})\in X_{k}
. There holds

\displaystyle\|(R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(\cdot,y_{0})\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}

uniformly for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{*}
, and all
y_{0}\in I_{S}\cup I_{W}
.

Proof.

As usual we define
g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}):=(R_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}f)(y,y_{0})
, where now

\displaystyle g_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z,y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{I_{3}^{c}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z,y_{0})}{(v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon)^{2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}).

Firstly, Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
show first
\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(I_{3}^{c}(y_{0}))}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}
and then
\|g_{2,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}
by means of the entanglement inequality. Secondly, we integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle g_{1,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{f(z,y_{0})}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\Big|_{\partial I_{3}(y_{0})}+\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}{\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)\frac{f(z,y_{0})}{v(z)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}\left(\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{5,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z.

Since
\left(\tfrac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)^{\prime}\in C^{1}
we appeal to Corollary
6.4
to get
\|g_{5,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim k^{-1}\|f\|_{X_{k}}
, while Proposition
4.1
, Corollary
4.3
and
f\in X_{k}
shows that
\|g_{3,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}
. As for
g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
, we recall

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v(y)-v(y_{0})\pm i\varepsilon}=\frac{(v^{\prime}(y_{0}))^{-1}}{y-y_{0}\pm i\varepsilon_{0}}-\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0}),

with
\mathrm{V}_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})\in L_{y}^{\infty}(0,2)
uniformly for all
y_{0}\in[0,2]
and all
\varepsilon>0
. Hence,

\displaystyle g_{4,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\frac{\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)}{\xi}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0},z)f(z,y_{0})\right)V_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=g_{6,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})+g_{7,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(y,y_{0})

We first address
g_{6,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
. We have

\displaystyle g_{6,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{4k^{2}}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1+2\gamma_{0}}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-1+2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4k^{2}}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-1}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{r}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})+\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{r}}(z,y_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4k^{2}}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

and we argue for the last two integral, since they are the most singular. Firstly, integrating by parts once more we have

\displaystyle\frac{4k^{2}}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-1-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=-\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2\gamma_{0}v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\Big|_{\partial I_{3}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2\gamma_{0}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

Using Proposition
4.1
and Corollary
4.3
, the boundary term is clearly bounded by
k^{-1}\|f\|_{X_{k}^{1}}
. As for the integral, we observe that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{2\gamma_{0}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\partial_{z}\left(\frac{\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})}{v^{\prime}(z)}\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2k\|f\|_{X_{k}^{1}}(1-2\mu_{0})\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}|\xi|^{-2\mu_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}^{1}}

from which the
X^{0}_{k}
bounds follows swiftly. The complete
X_{k}^{1}
bound is deduced similarly. Finally, note that

\displaystyle\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}

\displaystyle\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\right)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)\partial_{z}\left(f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

and since

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{z}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(\cdot,y_{0},z)\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim|\xi|,\quad\left|\partial_{z}\left(f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\right|\lesssim k|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{X_{k}^{1}}

we deduce from Proposition
4.1
, Proposition
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
that we can bound

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2k\|f\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}-2\mu_{0}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}

and similarly for the
\partial_{y}
derivative, so that we deduce

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z\right\|_{X_{k}^{1}}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{K}^{1}}.

The other four integrals are bounded following the same ideas, namely integrating by parts to reduce the singularity in
\xi
and using Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
, we omit the routine details. Likewise, note that for
g_{7,k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}
we shall only focus on two contributions. The first one is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1}{v^{\prime}(z)}\partial_{z}\left(\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\right)\xi^{1-2\gamma_{0}}V_{1,\varepsilon}^{\pm}(z,y_{0})\mathrm{d}z

which is bounded in
X_{k}^{1}
by
k^{-1}\|f\|_{X_{k}}
because of Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
and
\left|\xi^{1-2\gamma_{0}}\right|\lesssim 1+|\log(\xi)|

is integrable for
|\xi|
bounded. The second one is

\displaystyle\frac{2k}{v^{\prime}(y_{0})}\int_{I_{3}(y_{0})}\frac{1-2\gamma_{0}}{v^{\prime}(z)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{k,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\right)_{\mathsf{s}}(y,y_{0},z)f_{\mathsf{s}}(z,y_{0})\xi^{-2\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}z,

which is again bounded by Propositions
4.1
and
4.2
